# M6 plan: performance and production

- **Status:** approved by the maintainer on 2026-11-17; M6 is ready to start. The maintainer asked for the performance findings to become M6's work packages, as the earlier milestones' plans did with theirs. The same day they added four production areas: tools for a content team, the world's look beyond characters, animation, and audio, which this plan carries as five tracks (T, C, W, A and U, after the performance tracks). Then they added five more: foliage, water, weather, environment effects and particles, carried as tracks G, H, Y, V and N (after U), and decided that everything in this plan is M6's: nothing moves to a later milestone, and each track's minimal slice (§5) is the order its packages land in, not a cut. With that, they accepted the plan as M6's. Every other decision in §3 was taken by the agents who wrote it, and each can be undone by saying so (§7).
- **Milestone:** M6, which lands as release 0.3.0. It builds on 0.2.0 (tag `v0.2.0`): M0 to M5 built, M5 closed with its exit test 1 not met on Desktop-High by 0.03 ms of GPU frame on one valid round ([m6-handoff.md](m6-handoff.md)), nothing played by a person, and nothing run on a phone or on a Windows or Linux GPU.
- **Inputs:**
  - The spec: §3's tiers and budgets (all still proposals) and its measured sections (§3.5 to §3.8), the renderer's module spec (§5.12), §5.16 and §5.17's measured costs, and §9's gates.
  - [m6-handoff.md](m6-handoff.md): "What M6 inherits from the exit run", "Rules the reviews made" and "Everything deferred".
  - [m5-exit-run.md](m5-exit-run.md), "Final run at f4efa213e": the verdicts, their figures, the rule for a void round and what the run found.
  - The maintainer's performance research of 2026-11-16 and 17, kept outside the repository: a read of the code and of M5's final batch, ranking where the frame goes and what to change. This plan carries its findings in its own words. Its engine facts are cited here by file and line at `main` = `551977f30`, whose code is v0.2.0's (the commits after the tag change documents only), and its figures are cited to the tracked record or marked as §1 says.
  - The maintainer's research of 2026-11-17 on tools for a content team, the world's look, animation and audio, kept outside the repository: a read of the editor, the pipeline, the renderer and the animation and audio crates at the same commit, of how other engines and the reference games' studios work, and a design for each area with its packages, the seams between areas, an order, exit tests and the decisions it needs. The production tracks carry its findings in their own words, cite engine facts by file and line at `551977f30`, and name published sources in words, marked as §1 says. A later round the same day, also kept outside the repository, covered foliage, water, weather, environment effects and particles the same way, and read every row already in this plan to say which it keeps, amends or replaces; tracks G, H, Y, V and N and the amended rows carry it.

## 1. What M6 is for

M6 answers two questions. First: **can the engine hold its frame rows with headroom in the fight that ships, and can its measurements say where every millisecond goes?** Then: **can a content team (level designers, artists, animators and sound designers) make a zone that looks, moves and sounds like the reference games without writing Rust,** with its forests, seas, weather, atmosphere and effects?

On the first, 0.2.0 can't say either:

- its one fail is Desktop-High's district fight, 12.03 ms of GPU frame at p95 against 12.00, resting on one valid round ([m5-exit-run.md](m5-exit-run.md), "Final run at f4efa213e");
- that fight is a lower bound on the one a game ships: it plays no combat effect, no cut-in, no probe grid, no reflection probe and no daylight;
- the report reading the batch takes each statistic's least over every round, void rounds too, and Metal's pass rows are tails that overlap, so no figure today says what one pass costs;
- every phone figure is this Mac at a phone tier's settings.

M6 first makes the measurement able to judge the work, then measures the fight that ships, then cuts the frame where the measurement says, each change priced by a probe before it is built and stopped by a stated criterion if the price is too small. The performance tracks add no feature; the production tracks add what a content team needs ("What the production tracks add", below); neither adds a game (§3).

**Marks used in this plan:**

- **[M]** measured on the M5 Mac (an Apple M5, 24 GB) at the named tier's settings, size and cook. A measured figure cites its tracked record where it has one. One marked **[M, logs]** is read from M5's final batch's logs at f4efa213e, which are kept beside the batch outside the repository; B1's report prints such figures.
- **[E]** estimated from measured rows.
- **[U]** unverified: read from the code, a vendor's document or wgpu's changelog, and not measured here. The production tracks' published sources (an engine's or a vendor's documentation, a talk and its year) are named in words and carry this mark.

**Every phone figure is the Mac at phone settings,** never a phone's. On Metal a pass row is an exclusive tail, a pass's end minus the latest end before it (`crates/sapphire_render/src/graph/timing.rs:38-51`): rows sum to the frame, but a pass that runs beside another is charged only for what sticks out, so only the GPU frame is exact.

### Where the frame goes at v0.2.0

Every tier is GPU-bound on this Mac. At p95 the CPU frame is a quarter to two fifths of the GPU frame on each tier's dearest unit, and at most 2.98, 2.28, 1.30 and 1.48 ms against its 8, 12, 12 and 22 ms lines ([m5-exit-run.md](m5-exit-run.md), exit test 1). The unit nearest each line [M, logs, but for the GPU frame's p95, which is the runbook's], p50 / p95 in ms and the share of the p50 frame in brackets:

| Tier (GPU line) | Unit | GPU frame | Main pass | Depth only | Resolution-bound passes | Phones' output-size tail | CPU frame p95 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Desktop-High, 2560×1440 (12) | `district-fight`, round 2 | 11.15 / **12.03** | 5.81 / 6.65 (52%) | 2.14 / 2.38 (19%) | TAA 1.09, clustering and AO 0.99, post 0.87 (26%) | — | 2.98 |
| | `exit-walk`, the hour running, round 2 | 9.19 / 11.80 | 4.04 / 4.79 (44%) | 1.59 / 4.01 (shadows 3.70 at p95) | 2.98 (32%) | — | 2.29 |
| Desktop-Low, 1920×1080 (14) | `exit-walk`, round 1 | 6.24 / 8.61 | 2.71 / 3.58 (43%) | 1.33 / 2.88 (21%) | 1.72 (28%) | — | 2.18 |
| | `district-fight`, round 1 | 7.68 / 8.09 | 3.97 / 4.33 (52%) | 1.80 / 1.98 (23%) | 1.68 (22%) | — | 2.16 |
| Mobile-High, output 2532×1170 (13) | `district-fight`, round 2 | 4.64 / 4.91 | 2.42 / 2.66 (52%) | 0.85 / 0.91 (18%) | AO 0.26, post 0.29 (12%) | about 0.74 (16%) | 1.30 |
| Mobile-Low, output 2532×1170 (26) | `district-fight`, round 1 | 3.27 / 3.65 | 2.16 / 2.33 (66%) | 0.08 / 0.67 | post 0.19 (6%) | about 0.76 (23%) | 1.48 |

What that says:

- **The main pass is 42 to 66% of the GPU frame,** and in the fights about half of it is anime character shading. Between the district fight's ticks 301 and 801 on Desktop-High the main pass rises 2.21 ms for 623,000 more character pixels [M, logs]. Part of that rise doesn't scale with pixels, so a character pixel costs roughly 3 to 5 times the environment pixel it covers, and the characters are about 2.4 to 2.6 ms of the busiest frame's 6.16 ms main pass [E]. Counted from the shader, a body pixel by day does about 17 texture reads and 390 arithmetic operations with no local light: the largest groups are the shadow taps (9 cascade taps, 18 in a blend band, and 9 more for each received character's map) and the material maps' manual two-level reads.
- **Depth-only work is next:** the character prepass, the scene prepass and the cascades, 15 to 23% of the desktops' frames [M, logs], and 4.01 ms at p95 on Desktop-High's exit walk while the hour runs, when every light step redraws the cascades.
- **On the desktops the resolution-bound passes follow** (temporal AA, clustering with GTAO, post): 26 to 33% of Desktop-High's frame [M, logs].
- **On the phone tiers, the output-size upscaler tail** is 15 to 23% of the frame [M, logs]. Its two passes run at the output's size whatever the scene's: 0.73 to 0.74 ms in look-dev, 0.40 to 0.48 ms more than bilinear (spec §5.12, "As built (M5 L14)").
- **The CPU matters only through tails,** each a single frame, which a p95 never shows: a fight's restart at 7.85 to 7.88 ms and about 11,100 allocations [M, logs] (a median of 8.4 to 9.2 ms in the game, spec §3.5); a spring rig's entry at tick 1001, where joints go from 442 to 631 and secondary motion takes 1.57 ms on Desktop-Low against its 0.5 ms row and 1.53 on Mobile-Low against 1.0 [M, logs]; the texture streamer's worst, 0.815 ms on Desktop-Low against 0.5 [M, logs]; and frames whose thread sat on an efficiency core, 8 to 21% of the top 1% of `record` frames against at most 0.1% of the rest, among them a 28.2 ms Desktop-Low walk frame the report's least-of-rounds hid [M, logs].
- **What M5's features bought,** valid rounds only [M, logs]: capsule bins 0.8 to 1.5 ms a tier, the largest measured shading win; the held shadow cache 0.58 / 0.98 ms p50 / p95 on Desktop-Low; levels of detail 0.42 / 0.89 ms on Desktop-Low and at most 0.12 on the phones, for 0.08 to 0.23 ms of `render` CPU; character levels nothing measurable; draw runs 0.10 to 0.27 ms of `render` CPU and no GPU change. Geometry, submission and visibility convert poorly into time on this GPU; per-pixel character work converts well.

**What the benchmark can't see,** each able to hide a millisecond or more:

- **No combat effect:** `gpu_vfx_us` reads 0 on every traced frame [M, logs]; the spec's particles-and-distortion row is 1.2 ms on Desktop-Low and 3.0 on Mobile-Low (§3.3).
- **No cut-in:** measured outside the batch at f4efa213e, a cut-in adds 1.02 ms of GPU and 1.05 ms of CPU on Desktop-High at 1920×1080 while it plays (spec §5.17, L10's costs) [M].
- **No daylight:** the district's day has the sun at intensity 0 in both keys (`bench/district/district_day.ron`).
- **No probe grid and no reflection probe:** the generator writes `probes: None` and no reflections (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/bench/district.rs:450-462`). The grid's per-pixel lookup measured 0.6 to 1.0 ms of Desktop-High's 1080p frame in look-dev (spec §5.12, the ambient grid's "Where it goes") [M], about 1.1 to 1.8 ms at 1440p [E].
- **The phone tiers ran the desktop's CPU:** nine compute workers on this Mac, where a phone has its performance cores less one (`crates/sapphire_jobs/src/topology.rs:54-58`).
- **No present:** the bench waits for the GPU every frame (`apps/sapphire_sandbox/src/bench.rs:413`), so pacing, latency, input and heat aren't read.
- **No phone:** a phone's GPU is several times slower by a factor nobody has measured, so the phone ranking in §5 uses shares of the frame, not milliseconds.

Smaller distortions: twelve enemies on the phone tiers where their row says six (spec §3.4); zone walks keep Desktop-High's spring quality on every tier ([m6-handoff.md](m6-handoff.md), "The benchmark and its measurement"); the shipped game never turns the texture streamer on (the handoff's "Memory, streaming and patches"); the `kept` cascade mode was never timed; and the generated content flatters every row.

So **the measured fight is a lower bound.** With a probe grid's lookup on every frame and a cut-in on the frames it plays, Desktop-High's shipped fight lands near 13 to 15 ms at p95 [E], 1 to 3 ms over its line rather than 0.03, with effects, unmeasured, on top. B4 and B5 replace that estimate with a measurement.

### What a one-genre engine may assume, and what each buys

A general engine must shade any material under any light from any direction, filter every texture's continuous detail, and keep the world drawn behind any screen. An engine for anime-aesthetic action games may assume the opposite where the code shows the assumption carries weight. Only those a package uses are listed.

- **Characters are few, and they are the hero content:** 15 to 19% of a fight's pixels [M, logs], each dearer than an environment pixel. So the anime character shader and its hull can be cut term by term, the hero and close-ups pinned whole and the rest coarsened by pixel density (P1 to P5, F4, K3, K4).
- **Light is authored:** one key light on a schedule, ramps and bands, a light rig; another character's shadow is a soft darkening under the two-tone split. So the shadow light can latch (S1, S2), received shadows can be bounded, the hull can take one shadow tap, face light can be hoisted per character, and lesser lights can fold into a rig fill (P1 to P3, P6).
- **The frame is mostly flat colour regions with hard edges.** So flat maps can read one whole level (P4), the upscaler can take a one-tap path in flat groups (F1, F2), and a control map constant per region can fold into the material (K4).
- **Fights pause under menus, zone trips have wipes, and a restart can sit behind a fade.** So the scene can be held instead of drawn (F7), probes composed under the wipe (E4), and the restart spread behind the fade (J2).
- **Effects follow temporal AA's resolve and never enter its history.** So they can draw into a second output of the resolve with no whole-frame copy (F6, E1).
- **One lead is in close view,** and secondary motion's clearance pass is fitted to the lead's garment. So figures off the lead and the phone tiers can solve on coarser intervals (J1).

**Lines are the look,** so the same specialization rules things out. Variable-rate and half-rate shading staircase the two-tone split, which takes `fwidth` (`crates/sapphire_render/shaders/anime_character.wgsl:1333`), and moving lines keep no temporal history (`crates/sapphire_render/src/temporal.rs:40-51`), so nothing may lean on history for lines. The specialization buys no saving on a shaded pixel that a tuned general engine drawing this look wouldn't also make; its lead is in which pixels it may cheapen exactly, and in tails, hitches and power.

### What the production tracks add

The engine's runtime is further along than a list of gaps suggests; what is missing is mostly the layer between a person and the runtime. For each area, the gap as a read of the code at `551977f30` corrected it:

- **The editor (T).** Its foundations are built and tested and nobody has opened its window: a docked shell of 12 panes on egui 0.37, text documents with undo (fuzzed over 22,725 edits), a viewport 0 pixels from a game capture, 344 tests. Missing is the half a team touches: actions as named commands, undo across documents, IDs on placements (a selection is an index, `tools/sapphire_editor/src/app/zone.rs:28-34`), every zone object drawn and picked, cooking (`tools/sapphire_editor/src/cooked.rs:1-7`: it never cooks), a journal against a crash and the visual panes. Play-in-editor runs moves only (`tools/sapphire_editor/src/play.rs:11-14`).
- **The content pipeline (C).** The engine's half is done well: deterministic cached cooks, face maps from painted threshold masks (not from normals), OkLab ramps, control maps. The artist's half is absent: a Blender add-on, a budget check outside `cargo test`, cook on save, a character scaffold (the handoff's recipe copies `.meta` files by hand), variants, a mobile skeleton cut and a shared cache, whose key leaves out the architecture (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/cache.rs:94-148`). "No LFS" is a written decision (`.gitattributes:9-11`), and golden images are 791 of the history's 920 MiB.
- **The world's look (W).** The world's light exists (environment and foliage families, sky, fog, probes, wet surfaces); water, falling weather, terrain, screen-space reflections, impostors, photo mode, costume variants and the external upscalers don't. The phones don't want a temporal upscaler: they run 4× MSAA with no temporal AA, Mobile-Low never has a readable scene depth (`crates/sapphire_render/tiers.ron:263-264`, `:314-315`), and moving lines keep no history.
- **Animation (A).** Graphs partly exist: a layered locomotion state machine, 2D blend spaces, inertialized transitions and bone masks under a closed schema (`crates/sapphire_assets/src/graph.rs`), with IK, springs, cloth and faces. Missing are FBX clips that cook to the same bytes everywhere (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/clip.rs:22-24`), BVH and contacts, a graph an animator can grow, feet fitted to the controller, any rigid-body dynamics, any traversal (`GroundMode` has three states, `crates/sapphire_gameplay/src/controller/mod.rs:139-146`), directional reactions (the fight feeds a default, `game/game_combat/src/arena.rs:916`), and VRM's texture and colour binds.
- **Audio (U).** The seams are clean: a small backend trait, plain numbers a frame, bar-locked music, voice by language; and kira 0.12.6 already has a reverb and send tracks the engine doesn't use. Missing above kira are a per-voice filter (`VoiceParams` is gain, pan and pitch, `crates/sapphire_audio/src/backend.rs:22-30`), a mix document, priorities (32 voices, the 33rd request dropped, `crates/sapphire_audio/src/system.rs:21-23`), events richer than one random pick, rooms, occlusion, ambience, streaming, loudness and a voice-over pipeline. Every language's lines ship and load together (`crates/sapphire_stage/src/audio.rs:481-489`), and decoded sounds sit as stereo `f32` in no ledger (`crates/sapphire_audio/src/kira_backend.rs:197-203`).
- **Foliage (G).** The foliage family sways and bends to a trail (`crates/sapphire_render/src/foliage.rs:101-102`, `:335`), but every plant is an entity drawn with a 192-byte instance (`crates/sapphire_render/src/gpu_types.rs:220-244`) at one level (`crates/sapphire_render/src/levels.rs:48-52`), and nothing places, cooks or generates plants. W15 and W16 planned grass and impostors; a forest also needs placement rules cooked to the same bytes everywhere, a field of 16-byte instances culled by chunk, tree levels, shadow proxies, generated species and the tools to paint them.
- **Water (H).** W2 to W5 planned still water: volumes, a flat surface, a look under it and splashes. Oceans with waves and shores, rivers that flow and fall, caustics and floating things were in no row, and the engine has no water at all today.
- **Weather (Y).** Weather is two numbers, wetness and puddles (`crates/sapphire_look/src/weather.rs:19-24`), packed into the frame's uniforms with two lanes spare (`crates/sapphire_render/src/gpu_types.rs:93-95`); the wind is a renderer-only struct (`crates/sapphire_render/src/frame.rs:654-680`) that springs and cloth never see (`crates/sapphire_anim/src/springs.rs:1801`). W6 to W9 planned rain and lightning; there was no wind field, no weather state that schedules and blends, no snow, sand, hail or storm sky, and nothing accumulates or dries.
- **Environment effects (V).** Fog is one height fog whose density volumes are "later" (`crates/sapphire_render/src/fog.rs:92-99`), exposure is authored with no adaptation (`crates/sapphire_render/src/post.rs:287-288`), and the desktop tiers set a `volumetric_fog` flag that nothing reads (`crates/sapphire_render/tiers.ron:180`, `:230`; `crates/sapphire_render/src/quality.rs:249-251`). Snow and sand cover, ice, footprints, darkness and carried lights, light beams, a rainbow, an aurora and ambient motes don't exist.
- **Particles (N).** The effects crate simulates particles on the CPU, up to 4,096 on Desktop-High (`crates/sapphire_vfx/src/budget.rs`), as six generated shapes moved by speed, gravity and drag (`crates/sapphire_vfx/src/document.rs:43-130`), and only combat plays them (`game/game_combat/src/cues.rs:232`). The tiers promise 50,000 and 8,000 live GPU particles that nothing reads (`crates/sapphire_render/tiers.ron:184`, `:283`), and the iOS Simulator has no indirect draws (`crates/sapphire_rhi/src/caps.rs:381-389`). Missing are painted sheets, forces, events, ribbons and meshes as particles, a path for many cheap particles that every tier runs, effects placed in the world, and their budgets.

The production tracks hold 163 packages in 183 pull requests (a package split a, b or c lands as one pull request a part), about 89 agent-weeks [E] beside the performance tracks' 18 [E]. Each track names a minimal slice that still answers its area's question, 82 pull requests and about 42 agent-weeks in all [E] (§5). The slices are the order each track lands in, not a cut: every package in this plan is M6's, by the maintainer's decision. The constraint that binds is people and art, not code: every production exit test below can pass on this Mac, CI and the simulators with generated content, and each leaves owed the half only a level designer, an artist, an animator, a sound designer or a voice cast can give.

### Exit tests

Each is a proposal, judged on the M5 Mac as M5's were, alone on the machine with the display asleep, by the rule for a void round in [m5-exit-run.md](m5-exit-run.md) (or B1's reading, if §3's decision on it is taken). Each says what this run can prove and what it can't. **Owed in every test:** the phones, a Vulkan or DX12 GPU's exclusive pass times, and an M1 to M4 Mac.

1. **The measurement judges the work (new).** The report reads valid rounds only: each per-run table shows every round with its lock, void rounds marked, and a least-of-rounds figure only as a labelled diagnostic. A serial run times each pass alone on Metal. Frames the bench leaves out (a restart) are rows of their own. Every M5 unit that had no valid round has one (B0).
   - **Proved here:** the report's tables, re-read over M5's final logs with no verdict moving, and a serial run printing every pass.
   - **Owed:** exclusive pass times on a backend that doesn't overlap passes; on Metal a serial figure is a split of the frame, not a cost.
2. **Desktop-High's district fight holds with headroom (carried from M5's exit test 1).** `district-fight`'s GPU frame p95 is at or under **11.4 ms, 5% under its 12.0 ms row,** on each of two valid rounds, and every other tier's §3.2 and §3.3 rows still hold as M5's final run judged them. The 5% margin, 0.6 ms, is a proposal (§3): it is larger than the 0.1 to 0.5 ms gains that sit inside the run-to-run spread (§6), so a pass doesn't rest on luck.
   - **Proved here:** the Mac at Desktop-High's settings.
   - **Owed:** Desktop-High's reference device, and whether 5% is margin enough on it.
3. **The fight that ships is measured, and holds (new).** The district fight with combat effects and cues on, a cut-in casting the ultimate, in the district with a probe grid, reflection probes and the sun up (B4, B5), has rows on all four tiers, and each tier's GPU frame p95 is at or under its line: 12, 14, 13 and 26 ms.
   - **Proved here:** the Mac at each tier's settings, on the generated effects pack and cast.
   - **Owed:** a phone's frame; authored effects. Desktop-High's estimate today is 13 to 15 ms, so this is the test most likely to need §5's gate after batch 2.
4. **The CPU tails sit under their rows (new).** On a valid run, read at the worst frame and not at p95, on purpose, since the tails are the point: Desktop-Low's secondary motion under its 0.5 ms row (1.57 ms worst at v0.2.0) and Mobile-Low's under its 1.0 (1.53); Desktop-Low's texture streaming under its 0.5 ms row (0.815 worst); and no fight's restart holding a frame, so the restart's row (B3) reads no frame with more than 1 ms of restart in it (a proposal; 7.85 to 7.88 ms at v0.2.0). Every v0.2.0 figure here is [M, logs]; B3 puts them in the report.
   - **Proved here:** the Mac's cores at each tier's settings, with B7's phone-shaped pool beside the phone tiers.
   - **Owed:** a phone's cores, slower by a factor nobody has measured.
5. **The phones' frames fall (new).** Mobile-High's and Mobile-Low's district fight GPU frames fall at p50 by at least the share F1's and F2's probes price for the steps that land, a figure written into the runbook from B9 before the work lands, never a guess; F1's flat path is byte-exact, so no golden moves by it; and the edge upscaler's line gate passes (`look_tiers::the_edge_upscaler_beats_bilinear_at_each_phone_floor`).
   - **Proved here:** the Mac at the phone tiers' settings.
   - **Owed:** a phone's frame and its power, which the folded tail exists to save.
6. **The look holds (carried).** Every golden is byte-identical except where a package's row says it re-baselines, with the FLIP bound the row states. Outlines and the two-tone edge are unchanged at every level switch (`render::level_switches`, the testkit's gates), and the phones' line gate passes.
   - **Proved here:** Metal, lavapipe and WARP.
   - **Owed:** a person's eye on motion: S1's latched step, P4's snapped level, P5's switches, E2's cut-in aliasing.
7. **No hitches and no pipeline growth (carried).** At the commit the final batch measures: `zero_hitches` cold and warm on Metal; the district fight cold and warm (`the_district_encounter_plays_with_no_hitch_cold_and_warm`); the sandbox's replays tests on Metal; and the ten "How to add" recipes walked with no Rust step. M5 ran these last at its rehearsal, not at the commit it measured. Each tier's pipeline inventory is no larger than at v0.2.0 (B0 records it) plus what a row declares, and the cold warm-up is no slower. The production tracks' new pipelines count against the same baseline by their rows (§5, "Pipelines"), and `zero_hitches` also passes cold and warm with rain starting, a camera going under water and photo mode opening, and with a storm starting, lightning's first strike, snow cover starting, a trip into a zone with an ocean, a cave entered, an aurora night, the first world effect and the first kind of mote. Z1 also times a cold boot into W1's arm with every feature on, on the iOS Simulator and the Android emulator, and the warm-up stays inside its budget.
   - **Proved here:** Metal on this Mac, and lavapipe in CI by counts.
   - **Owed:** a phone driver's cold compile.
8. **Patches stop holding the boot screen (new).** With packs capped (K2), the 5.6 MB patch that held the boot screen 5.4 to 5.6 s against the district's 1.12 GB pack (M5's M7 spike) holds it under 0.6 s. The target is an estimate; the spike's script measures it.
   - **Proved here:** the Mac, from a loopback host.
   - **Owed:** a phone's storage, and a patch over a real network.

Tests 9 to 15 are the production tracks': one an area for T, C, A and U; test 11 for the world's look, foliage and water (W, G, H) and the weather and atmosphere W carries; test 14 for particles (N); test 15 for a day of weather and atmosphere (Y, V). Each passes on this Mac, CI and the simulators with generated content; none needs a person, a device or an account to pass here, and each names the judgement it leaves owed. Since the slices land first (§5), a clause whose package hasn't landed waits for it; none is dropped, because nothing is cut.

9. **A zone is built and previewed in the editor, with no Rust and no hand-edited text (new).** A committed command script (T1), run headless, creates a zone with its colliders and probes, places 20 prefabs snapped to the ground, adds lights, a reflection probe, a spawner, a trigger, a checkpoint, an exit and an encounter, cooks, plays it in the game child (T19) and saves: the cook refuses nothing, the child's replay reaches its checksum on three OSes, `git diff` holds only documents and their sidecars, and undoing every step gives back the empty zone's bytes. The effects, look (four tiers), sequencer (a zone cutscene in its zone) and graph panes each differ from a stage capture by 0 pixels after a scripted edit (the renderer's known one-code flicker allowed), undo gives back the bytes and the pixels, no edit or tier change builds a pipeline on first use, and the editor's rows (T12) are measured.
   - **Proved here:** the Mac; CI's three OSes for the headless half.
   - **Owed:** a person building a zone at a window, and a level designer's, an artist's and an animator's sessions; Windows and Linux windows; a two-person merge on a real pair of branches.
10. **An authored character goes from a modelling tool to the screen (new).** The project-made test character, built by a headless Blender script, goes through the add-on's export (C3), `check` at the hero role (C1), `new-character` (C5), an anti-aliased face map (C6), both cooks, a `--capture` look-dev run and the simulators through live link; a re-export shows through `content --watch` and hot reload without a restart (C4); every step is a command in C16's recipe. If the maintainer names a cache host (§7), a second machine also cooks from it (C10, C11): a fresh checkout hits every asset but the other architecture's textures, its packs byte-identical to a cook without the cache, and a planted stage change without a version bump fails `--verify-hits`.
   - **Proved here:** the Mac; CI cooking and checking the fixtures on three OSes; the simulators.
   - **Owed:** an artist's own character (painted control maps, real hair); Blender on Windows and Linux; the cache over a real network.
11. **The world's look holds its rows and stays presentation (new).** W1's `nature-walk`, `nature-rain` (with its storm state, and its blizzard and sandstorm arms), `nature-fight`, `nature-forest` and `nature-coast` (with its dive arm), two valid rounds each on four tiers, with water and waves, terrain (on §3's default), grass, trees in the foliage field with their levels and impostors, rain, screen-space reflections, cover and fog volumes on as their rows set them: each unit's GPU frame p95 is at or under 11.4 ms on Desktop-High (exit test 2's margin) and its tier's line on the others (14, 13 and 26 ms); each new row is within 1.5× its proposal; the CPU rows are unchanged but for the new foliage, water and weather rows, each within its proposal, and grass and scatter placement's activation frames, which stay inside the streaming row; and draws stay under §3.4's (2,500 on desktop, 700 on phones), with foliage's at or under 150, 150, 60 and 40. Placement and scatter cooks are byte-identical across thread counts and machines, and the wave twin is within 1e-3 m of the GPU. Every committed replay reaches its checksum with every W, G, H, Y and V feature on and off; a swim replay reaches the same checksum on arm64 and x86-64 with storm waves shown; every shipped zone's digest is unchanged except a zone with water bodies, terrain or foliage colliders, each by its row. The recipes "How to add a plant and a forest" (G8) and "How to add zone weather" (Y6) pass in CI.
   - **Proved here:** the Mac at each tier's settings; CI's counted walks; the iOS Simulator and the Android emulator walking the `ci` nature zone; byte stability and digests in CI.
   - **Owed:** a phone's frame and a discrete PC's; an artist's eye on water, rain, terrain, grass, trees, snow, sand and ice against the reference games.
12. **Motion comes in clean, traversal holds in the simulation, and reactions and faces read (new).** A synthetic BVH (an A-pose rest, 30 fps, noise, an injected slide) and a Blender-written FBX cook onto two characters with slide under 5 mm on planted ticks, the same bytes on three OSes, and an FBX-sourced move bakes pinned tables (A1 to A4); the shipped graphs run on v2 with every golden and replay unmoved (A5c); locomotion from 0.5 to 6 m/s slides under 2 cm a stride (A7). The `course` zone's replays (mantle, climb and glide, and grapple and swim where they land) reach their checksums on three OSes with no allocation in a step. A burst of enemy defeats in B5's fight stays inside the Physical animation row on four tiers with its caps holding; a hit from behind plays its variant; a texture-swap expression and a colour bind draw on four tiers with every other golden unmoved; a Japanese and an English line move one face's mouth by their phonemes.
   - **Proved here:** the Mac at each tier's settings; CI's three OSes.
   - **Owed:** captured motion and an animator's clips; feel, which nobody has judged for any of this engine's motion; a phone's CPU for the ragdoll row; recorded voice in each language.
13. **The fight is heard through its mix, and a line is heard in a second language (new).** Offline renders of `arena-boss`, the ward's walk with its conversation and the district fight with `--cues`, at the Home and Portable profiles: within ±2 LU of the target with sample peak at or under −1 dBFS; music and effects at least the mix's rule under every voice line; under a 200-request storm, dialogue and the top priorities play and every other request is counted; a room change and a virtual voice's revival make no click; occlusion closes a voice behind a block; the audio update, mix load and decoded PCM under their rows; nothing allocated per frame or on the audio thread; every replay's checksum unchanged with audio on and off. From a loopback host, an install with `vo_en` switches its voice to `ja`, fetches only `vo_ja`, plays the guide's greeting in Japanese with its mouth curve and a caption lasting its length, deletes `vo_ja` and falls back to `en`, counted; the recording script lists every voiced key in both languages.
   - **Proved here:** the Mac; CI's offline renders; the simulators by counters; a loopback host.
   - **Owed:** a person listening, and a sound designer's judgement of the mix and the rooms; recorded voices in two languages; a phone's speaker, headphones and Bluetooth; a real network.
14. **Effects at scale hold their rows and stay presentation (new).** `fight-effects-heavy` (B5's district fight playing N11's library) and `nature-storm-effects`, two valid rounds each on four tiers: combat effects within the combat particles row, world effects (motes included) within the world row, and the CPU's effects simulation within §3.2's row. Every v4 library read under v5 simulates bit-identically over 600 steps; the stateless path's goldens are byte-stable at 30 and 60 frames a second for the same world time; hitstop holds combat effects and not world effects; the iOS Simulator and the Android emulator draw the stateless path with no indirect draw; `zero_hitches` passes with the first world effect and the first kind of mote; N12's pane differs from a capture by 0 pixels on each tier; and no replay's checksum moves with effects on or off.
   - **Proved here:** the Mac at each tier's settings; CI's goldens and the version test; the simulators.
   - **Owed:** painted sheets from a VFX artist; a phone's fill on a device; the feel of stepped timing, by the maintainer's eye.
15. **A day of weather and atmosphere is presentation and holds its rows (new).** A scripted 24-hour walk at 600× in W1's zone through the schedule's states (clear, rain, a storm with lightning, snow, a blizzard, clear again, with a sandstorm arm), crossing a cave's volume and its darkness, a valley's mist and a frozen pond, and ending on a lamp-lit night with fireflies and an aurora: captures are byte-stable run to run at a fixed seed; accumulation is within 1e-4 of the CPU reference at each checkpoint; the wind sampler is within 1e-5 between GPU and CPU; every replay's checksum is unchanged with weather on, off and mid-transition; zone digests are unchanged; `zero_hitches` passes cold with a storm starting, a snow region entered, the first strike, a cave entered and an aurora night; and the storm (about 0.55, 0.5, 0.55 and 0.35 ms [E]) and the snowy night (about 0.66 ms on Desktop-High and 0.22 on Mobile-Low [E]) are within 1.5× their sums of rows.
   - **Proved here:** the Mac at each tier's settings; CI's scripted walk; the simulators for warm-up.
   - **Owed:** an artist's eye on snow, sand, ice, mist and darkness; a phone's heat through a long storm; a phone's warm-up on the device.

**Feel stays unknown.** Nobody has played anything, and M6 doesn't change that: its traversal, reactions and faces are proved by checksums, counts and pixels. Everything it builds serves any game on this engine.

## 2. What "done well" means

Ranked. Each has a measure a test, a bench or the report can read.

| # | Pillar | Measure |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Measured before built.** Every package names the measured row it acts on, a probe that prices it before the work, and a kill criterion that stops it. A killed package lands its measurement and an "As built: killed" note, and that counts as done. | Spec §3.9 (B9) holds every probe's rounds and the verdict it gave each package. |
| 2 | **Judged on the frame.** Gains are judged on `gpu frame` p95 from valid rounds first, since Metal's pass rows overlap and a saving can move into the next row; a pass row is read beside it, never alone. | B1's per-round tables. Exit tests 2 and 3. |
| 3 | **The look holds.** An exact change proves it with byte-identical goldens. A change that can't be exact says so in its row, re-baselines only the scenes it names, and states its FLIP bound. Lines and the two-tone edge are never traded. | Exit test 6. |
| 4 | **Nothing hitches and nothing grows.** No package adds a shipping pipeline without a row that declares it to the inventory and the warm-up; a measurement switch adds none. | Exit test 7. |
| 5 | **Phones by share.** A package for the phones names its share of the frame at phone settings, and the device test that would settle it (X3). | Every phone row's "Owed". |
| 6 | **The benchmark's flattery stated.** The generated cast paints its maps constant or graded per region, so a package that relies on that (K4 most of all) says so, and says what authored content would change. | Z3's review of the benchmark. |
| 7 | **Presentation moves no checksum.** | No committed replay moves with any package on or off. |

The production tracks' pillars rank after these, so where one meets a performance pillar the performance pillar wins; among themselves:

| # | Pillar | Measure |
|---|---|---|
| 8 | **The simulation keeps owning what moves a checksum.** Position, timing, hit volumes and traversal are the simulation's; the pose, faces, every physical reaction, weather, wind, waves, snow and sand cover, effects, reflections and sound are presentation. Physics in presentation only: ragdolls run on a presentation solver and move no replay. A checksum or a zone digest moves only by a row that says so (A14's traversal, W2's water, W12's terrain collision, A18's anchors, G1b's tree colliders), each zone's digest moving only where it gains that data. | A test flips each new checksum field; a digest test over every shipped zone; every replay with each presentation feature on and off (exit tests 11 to 15). |
| 9 | **Every production cost is a row.** A feature that costs frame time, CPU, memory or cook time has a proposed row in spec §3, a counter (R9) and a unit that prices it, and declares its pipelines in its row. None lands inside a timed window unless its unit is in B9's manifest. | W1's units, B5's fight, the offline renders, the cook-time rows; exit test 7's inventory. |
| 10 | **The pane equals the capture.** Every authoring surface is proved by pixels: after a scripted edit a pane differs from a stage capture of the saved documents by 0 pixels, and undo gives back the bytes and the pixels. Every action is a named command, so a session runs, records and replays headless. | Exit test 9; each T row's "Done when". |
| 11 | **No Rust step for content.** A zone, a character, a clip, a graph, a cue, a mix, a voiced line in another language and a look are made from documents and tools; one rule set (C1's check) is shared by the add-on, the cooker and CI. | Exit tests 9 and 10; the "How to add" recipes walked with no Rust step (exit test 7), C16's among them. |
| 12 | **Documents that merge.** Content stays text where it can; editor data (layout, comments) never enters a runtime document; placements have IDs and a zone's dressing can split into layer files; a binary tile has region undo. | T7's merge corpus; T8a and T8b's byte-identical cooks. |
| 13 | **Judgement owed, and said.** Generated content proves cost and correctness, not beauty or feel; each production exit test names who owes the rest. | Every production test's "Owed". |
| 14 | **One of each shared thing.** One wind field (Y1), one weather state (W8a), one set of accumulated shares (Y2), one volume shape (`ZoneShape`), one stateless particle path (N8a's include), one ring include, one interaction buffer (W15b) and one owner for zone-placed effects (N9). A package that needs one of these reads it; it never grows its own. | Each owner's row names its readers, and §5's shared-files table orders them; Z3's review lists any second copy found. |
| 15 | **Every tier runs it, or says what it shows instead.** No compute pass and no indirect draw is the main path for anything, since the iOS Simulator has neither indirect execution nor base vertex (`crates/sapphire_rhi/src/caps.rs:381-389`); a tier's difference is a pipeline variant or an override constant, never a uniform branch; each feature names its path or its stated absence on each of the four tiers. | Each row's tier caps; the simulators drawing each new golden; exit tests 14 and 15. |

## 3. Decisions this plan takes

Where a decision changes what the spec says, the spec says so with the package that builds it, as a "Decision" bullet in its section. Each can be undone as its last column says.

| Topic | Decision | Why | To undo |
|---|---|---|---|
| **What M6 is** | Performance at the budgets, and nine production areas in ten tracks beside it: the editor (T), the content pipeline (C), the world's look (W), animation (A), audio (U), foliage (G), water (H), weather (Y), environment effects (V) and particles (N). **Every package in this plan is M6's:** nothing moves to a later milestone, and each track's minimal slice (§5) is the order its packages land in, not a cut. No game content and no fight tuned. The game systems' track waits (the simulation's data from packs, a save across zones, a run-wide `tables_hash`), and the rest of the handoff's deferred list waits unless a track takes it ("From m6-handoff's 'Everything deferred'", below). Production work lands beside the performance batches but never inside a timed window, and the GPU look features wait for §5's gate. | The maintainer asked for the performance findings to become M6's packages, then on 2026-11-17 added the first four production areas, then foliage, water, weather, environment effects and particles, and decided that the whole of it is M6 and approved the plan. M5 left one row missed and a lower bound for a fight, so the measurement comes first: a feature that lands before the probes that judge it measures a moving target. | The maintainer's alone: drop a track, or invoke §5's cut order as a last resort; the batches take what remains. |
| **Measure first** | Batch 1 is measurement and the changes that need none. Every other package names a B9 run or a B-track unit that prices it, and its kill criterion binds: a package that misses it lands its measurement and stops. | Each estimate in §4 carries a range of a factor of two or more, and several rest on a model of the main pass that only a probe can check. | Build on the estimates, at the risk of work that buys nothing. |
| **How a gain is judged** | On `gpu frame` p95 from valid rounds first; a pass row is read beside it as a tail. A verdict that a test rests on needs two valid rounds (exit test 2). Gains of 0.1 to 0.5 ms are read from arms alternated in one sitting. | On Metal a saving in one pass can vanish under another's overlap. M5's one fail rested on one valid round, and its rounds of one unit differed by more than the fail's 0.03 ms. | One round, as M5's rule had it. |
| **Void rounds** | The fixed 0.10 lock rule stays the verdicts' rule until B0's rounds are in. B1 prints each round's lock beside its floor for the run's slow frames (about 1/√N by chance alone) and a significance reading; once B0 shows the two agree on every long unit, verdicts read significance. | The phones' short units read 0.10 to 0.22 by chance with the display asleep, so the fixed rule voids honest rounds ([m6-handoff.md](m6-handoff.md), "A round is void on a fixed lock"). Changing a rule before the leftover rounds are read would change M5's record. | Keep the fixed rule. |
| **Desktop-High's margin** | Exit test 2's 11.4 ms is 5% under the 12.0 ms row. | A pass or a fail by 0.03 ms is luck; 0.6 ms is more than the spread between rounds that the gains in §4 must clear. | Another margin, or none. |
| **The fight that ships is the fight measured** | B4 and B5 add probes, the sun, effects and a cut-in to the benchmark, and exit test 3 judges that fight. If B5 reads Desktop-High over its line by more than batch 3's priced packages can recover, §5's gate asks the maintainer to choose (§7). | A lower bound can't prove a budget, and the estimate puts the shipped fight 1 to 3 ms over. | Judge `district-fight` alone, as M5 did. |
| **What the benchmark proves** | Cost, not beauty, as in M5. A shortcut that relies on generated content's flatness says so in its row. | Generated figures paint their control maps exactly constant per region (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/figure/paint.rs:131-142`), so the benchmark under-represents what authored texture detail costs. | An authored district and cast. |
| **Phones stay estimates** | Every phone figure is the Mac at phone settings and is ranked by share. No package's go or kill rests on a phone millisecond. X3 makes the phone session one command, and the session itself is owed. | No phone has run anything, and the phone's limit (vertices, pixels or bandwidth) is unknown and decides several packages. | A phone (§7). |
| **Measurement switches** | B6's switches exist in `dev` builds alone. A switch in a shader is a pipeline-override constant whose default is the shipping pipeline's, never a uniform branch, and a test holds the shipping inventory's keys unchanged. | An override constant at pipeline build is the exact switch; a uniform branch around new code in the untaken path moved six goldens by up to 13 code values in L4 ([m6-handoff.md](m6-handoff.md), "Known limits and risks"). | Uniform switches, re-proving every golden. |
| **No pipeline without a row** | A package may add a shipping pipeline only if its row declares it, with the inventory, the warm-up and `declares_every_setting`; exit test 7 counts them against v0.2.0. Shader-permutation splits by a cooked material property (hair styles, layer sets as pipeline constants) are out while exit test 7 forbids hitches, unless a register report first shows an occupancy loss (§7's offline compilers). | The boot already prewarms 73 to 89 pipelines a tier (spec §3.7), and every new key is a cold compile on a phone. | A row that pays for its pipelines. |
| **The cooker** | Built where the audit (K1) and the measurements say, by the rules below. K1 and K2 first; K3 and K4 on their probes; the rest on triggers. | Almost none of the cost that misses a line is in the cooker's reach: of the character pixel's 17 reads by day, 11 are shadow and rim depth taps no cooked asset changes [counted from the shader]. M5's cooked features bought little: levels of detail 0.42 / 0.89 ms on Desktop-Low and at most 0.12 on phones, character levels nothing measurable, cell levels at most 0.2 ms [M, logs] for 13 to 17% more mesh pool ([m5-plan.md](m5-plan.md), D6's row). | Cook first and measure after. |
| **Pins** | wgpu 30.0.2 (X1), before any timed probe. egui, bevy and `sdl3-sys` as M5 left them. | 30.0.2 fixes a use-after-free at device loss that the streamer's mapped staging can meet. | Stay on 30.0.1. |
| **Sizes** | S is hours to a day of an agent's work, M a few days, L a week or more. | The findings are sized so; L rows split when they're briefed. | None needed. |
| **Accounts and downloads** | None. Agents download no tool, motion capture, art, audio or SDK: the offline shader compilers' counts, the DLSS SDK and every licensed asset are the maintainer's to provide (§7). Test content is generated: a test character by a headless Blender script, a synthetic BVH, a nature zone, synthesized voice lines and tones. | The run's rules; the one public motion dataset is non-commercial and no-derivatives (LAFAN1's licence [U]). | The maintainer's. |
| **CI** | Timed runs never run in CI. B9's manifest runs on the Mac, by the orchestrator, in quiet windows. New switches and counters get unit and GPU tests that run in CI as usual. | CI's caches are full and its rasterizers time nothing. | None needed. |
| **Production costs** | Every production feature that costs frame time, memory or cook time gets a proposed row in spec §3 (W0 writes the world's and those of G, H, Y and V, N0 the particles' split, A0 the Physical animation row, U1 the audio rows, T12 the editor's, C12 the cook's), a counter and a unit: W1's nature zone for the world's look, foliage, water, weather and atmosphere, with an area reserved for each track to fill; B5's fight for ragdolls and, with N11's library, for combat effects; the `course` replays for traversal; offline renders for audio. A nature zone spends its rows instead of the district's building load, so exit tests 2 and 3 keep judging the district and W1's units get lines of their own. | A cost nobody measures can't be judged against the performance work beside it. | Build without rows, judging each feature by eye. |
| **The editor, and its process model** | The editor grows into the content team's tool, on egui 0.37 and egui_tiles in `sapphire_editor`, owning the three widgets it lacks (a gizmo, a node canvas, a curve widget; no new crate). Authoring previews stay in the editor's process, each on its stage route; "Play in game" starts the `dev` sandbox as a child process over live link and pushes every save. Every action is a named command. | The pane equals the capture only through the in-process stage route (0 pixels, `tools/sapphire_editor/tests/viewport_stage.rs`); the in-process run is held to moves by feature unification (`play.rs:11-14`); a child keeps a game crash away from unsaved documents, as Godot's and Fyrox's editors chose [U: their documentation]. The ecosystem's node and gizmo crates still require egui 0.36 [U: crates.io]. | The editor stays a viewer: T5 onward drop, T0 to T4 stand. |
| **Audio, on kira** | Audio is a full track built above kira 0.12.6, `lewton` and `vorbis_rs`, all already in the tree: an engine voice on kira's `Sound` trait, a mix document, events, rooms on kira's reverb, voice-over packs. No Wwise, FMOD, Steam Audio, convolution reverb or Opus; HRTF and ambisonics neither. | kira has the reverb, sends and compressor this needs [U: kira's documentation]; middleware sits behind accounts and native builds a public repository and its CI can't carry; nobody has heard the engine's audio, so a mix and a loudness gate come before acoustics. | Audio waits for a later milestone: U drops, and A24 with it. A private game may still put middleware behind `AudioBackend`. |
| **Terrain inside zones** (reopening M5's "terrain out", [m5-plan.md](m5-plan.md) §3, "The target") | Height-field tiles per zone cell, drawn by one shared patch mesh as a CDLOD quadtree, with a packed 32-bit control map read by `textureLoad` (the layout Godot's Terrain3D documents [U]), a layer array for terrain alone, collision as a new `StaticShape::HeightField` and the nav bake over it. Backdrop mountains stay cooked meshes. A seamless open world, world partition and streaming beyond a zone stay out. | A 4 km zone at 1 m as cooked meshes is about 270 to 340 MB of level-0 vertices against 32 MiB of 16-bit heights [E], and a sculpt edits a tile where a mesh would recook. The layer array is a narrow exception to "no texture arrays" (below): fixed in size per cook, resident whole and never streamed, so the streamer's texture replacement never meets it. | Terrain stays out: W12, W13, W14 and T13b drop, and grass places by G1's rules on meshes. |
| **Traversal, and how far** | Traversal reopens inside zones in tiers. Tier 1 (sprint, mantle, vault) and tier 2 (climbing on tagged surfaces, gliding) are in M6; grapples to authored anchors and swimming in water volumes land only after A16b. It is simulation: a `TraversalMode` beside `GroundMode` in the checksum, traversal moves as baked tables with a vertical warp. A14's one re-record of the replays its layout and `controller_profiles_hash` refuse is accepted, in one pull request with the new `course` replays. | Movement is the controller's, so traversal can't be presentation. Tagged surfaces bound the content: shipped games made some surfaces unclimbable on purpose, and a studio cut free climbing as not core [U: Ubisoft's parkour overview; a report of a GDC 2026 talk]. | Tier 1 only (A16 to A18 drop), or none (A14 to A20 drop, and W2's water serves only the look). |
| **Ragdolls** | Enemy death falls and powered heavy-hit reactions run on an XPBD rigid-body solver of the engine's own in `sapphire_anim`, presentation only and capped per tier; playable characters keep authored reactions and defeats. A0 replaces spec §5.14's "swap in Jolt (via `joltc`)" and M5's "Ragdolls: None" with this. | The engine already runs XPBD cloth (3.7 µs an iteration for 128 links, spec §5.13); Rapier allocates every step and its cross-platform determinism needed a feature that moved three goldens (`crates/sapphire_physics/src/lib.rs:7-21`); Jolt or Avian would be a second physics stack. In presentation a ragdoll moves no checksum, and scalar maths keeps its goldens identical on three architectures [E]. | No physical motion (A10 to A13 drop, A8 carries reactions), or a crate (`rapier3d`, Jolt through `joltc`, `avian3d`), each a yes in §7. |
| **Hit direction** | Presentation chooses a reaction's direction variant from `HitEvent`'s point and normal (`crates/sapphire_gameplay/src/hits/mod.rs:70-90`); the simulation gains no field. | No checksum moves, and brains need no "hit from behind" fact yet. | A simulation field, moving checksums. |
| **Graph v2** | The graph becomes a compiled node document; shipped graphs convert in one pull request whose output is byte-identical (A5c), and v1's reader stays one milestone. Editor layout lives in a sidecar the cook ignores. Motion matching stays out. | A bit-for-bit conversion is the only proof v2 changes nothing; a layout field would recook a graph on a drag and put editor data in a runtime type. Motion matching needs hours of captured coverage [U: a GDC 2016 talk]. | Keep v1 beside v2. |
| **Version control for artists' sources** | The maintainer's choice. Until they make it, content stays in plain git and C15 waits. The recommendation, when an artist joins: a separate content repository on Git LFS with locking while there are fewer than about five artists and 100 GB, then Perforce or Unity Version Control at scale, or Perforce from the start if the studio uses it. `.blend` files, PSDs and WAV masters live there, never in this repository; until then only cooked fixtures are committed. Moving golden images out of git (86% of the history's bytes) is unchanged in M6, and revisited with this choice. | The engine is ready for any of them: identity is the GUID in `.meta`, and keys leave out paths. GitHub bills LFS storage and downloads, Actions' downloads included [U: GitHub's billing documentation], so engine CI never pulls art. | Name another system; C15 follows it. |
| **The pipeline's shape** | No new crate and no tool in the cook path: `sapphire_pipeline` gains `check`, `index`, `scaffold`, `variant`, `skeleton` and `remote`. The Blender add-on is MIT, built as a zip by CI and not on Blender's extensions site, supports Blender 5.2 LTS only, and calls the pipeline binary for every rule. `check` waivers are allowed in a sidecar with a written reason, each listed in the cook report. Mobile skeletons are cut at cook, dropping the deepest spring-chain joints. No Maya or 3ds Max exporter: their FBX goes through the same check. Import presets are copied into a sidecar when it is made, never inherited from folders. | One definition of a triangle or a joint, in Rust, after merges, levels and caps. Importing `.blend` in the cook would put Blender's version in every key and a GPL program in the cook path; the extensions site asks for a GPL licence [U: Blender's manual]. Folder defaults would make a key read files beyond its source. | A GPL add-on on the extensions site; waivers that block; two rigs authored per character. |
| **Seams between tracks** | Each seam has one owner a half: terrain edits live in `sapphire_zone::terrain::edit`, a runtime crate the editor and the running game link (W14), and W12's packed control map is the tile format; a graph's layout is a sidecar (A5a) and A9's debug snapshot is the trace the graph pane reads; the editor's audition belongs to U12a, T18 being the pane's slot; costume variants are C9's documents, which a playable kind names (W19 renders them); water volumes are in the zone's simulation half (W2) and waves stay presentation; visemes are mapped at cook (U14 with A24's tables); A6 owns surface tags, in the zone's presentation half; K1 owns the cook audit and C2 the index file. The later round's seams: the wind field is Y1's, in `sapphire_look`; the weather state is W8a's; the accumulated shares (wetness, puddles, snow, sand, ice) are Y2's; `ZoneShape` is `sapphire_zone`'s, landed by the first of V1 and W8a; the stateless particle include is N8a's, read by W6 and N8b; the ring include is written by the first of W7 and H6; the interaction buffer is W15b's, V8a's press a channel in it; zone-placed effects are N9's, H3b's falls and V9's ambience among them; the shed leaf rule is G5's, drawn by N. Asset kind numbers (31 is next; the new kinds land as Foliage, Scatter, Effects, Weather, Ambience and biome looks, in landing order), the zone's cooked version (9 since M6 W2, bumped once a landing), the live-link protocol (6) and the save (4) move in landing order, never reserved in the plan. | The research's tracks were written in parallel and proposed five seams from both sides, and the later round's five tracks each proposed a wind, a state or a shape of their own; these are its rulings. A tools crate can't hold terrain edits, since nothing may depend on a tool (`xtask/layers.toml:11`). | Another owner per seam, said in the row it moves. |
| **Smaller defaults** | Placement IDs are short random IDs with an optional typed name. T7's merge driver is installed by a documented `git config` line each clone runs, never by a hook. `WeatherDocument` lives in `sapphire_tod::weather`, its resolved `WeatherFrame` in `sapphire_look` beside `Weather`. Impostors bake hemi-octahedral at 12 × 12 views with the cooker's existing ray caster (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/probes/bvh.rs`), deterministic. A chosen look bumps the save's version once, and an old save takes the base look. The iOS Photos usage string for photo mode is added, in English and Japanese. The engine reads a neutral phoneme sidecar per line; which aligner made it is the studio's choice. The iOS audio session stays `Ambient`. | Merge safety; a hook would run unasked; layering (`sapphire_look` has no serde or reflection, `crates/sapphire_look/Cargo.toml`, and `sapphire_tod` already has both); cooker rule 8 (a GPU bake isn't byte-identical across vendors), and the probes' ray caster already bakes cut-out, two-sided geometry (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/probes/bake.rs:25-40`), so no second rasterizer; the save's migration rule; App Store review; no tool in the cook path. | Each, by saying so. |
| **No temporal upscaler on the phones** | The phones keep the edge upscaler (F1, F2); MetalFX's spatial scaler is only a spike against it (W17b, on §7's yes). No XeSS, FSR 3 frame generation or Arm's mobile upscaler; MetalFX temporal and DLSS on the desktops wait on §7. | The phones run 4× MSAA with no temporal AA, Mobile-Low has no readable depth, lines keep no history, and Arm's mobile derivative of FSR 2 costs 2.0 to 6.1 ms at 2× on current Mali GPUs [U: Arm's library documentation]. XeSS's licence is closed; frame generation adds a frame of latency and interpolates lines. | A phone measurement under 1 ms with lines intact. |
| **Loudness** | A look-ahead master limiter at −1 dBFS; `Home` at −24 LUFS ±2 and `Portable` at −18 ±2, chosen by platform and route; a player's Dynamic range setting (Wide, Standard, Night). | Sony's ASWG-R001 targets as Audiokinetic's mastering series reports them [U]; nobody has measured this game's loudness. | Other targets, once a listener judges (phone speakers may want −16 [U]). |
| **Voice languages** | Per line, the chosen voice language, then the base language, then subtitles only, each fallback counted, in place of spec §5.15's "no fallback"; a Voice language row apart from Language. Voice ships as `vo_<locale>` packs mounted only for the chosen language, the base install holding one; other languages are optional downloads through the engine's patcher after K2, with sizes and a delete. | The reference games ship voice packs as optional downloads [U: a player's guide]; 10,000 lines are about 180 to 240 MB a language [E]; a missing line should still say something. Store policy on such downloads is the maintainer's to check before a store build (§7). | Every language in the base pack, as now. |
| **One wind and one weather state** | One wind field: Y1's `WindField` in `sapphire_look`, with today's gust term moved verbatim into a shared `wind.wgsl`, wind volumes (directional, vortex, burst; 8, 8, 4 and 0 on the GPU a tier, 8, 8, 4 and 4 on the CPU) in one 832-byte `WindBuffer`, sampled the same way in Rust and WGSL; foliage, precipitation, effects (N2's Wind force), springs and cloth (Y3) all read it. One weather state: W8a's `WeatherDocument` in `sapphire_tod::weather`, resolved to a `WeatherFrame` in `sapphire_look` by a `WeatherSystem` in `sapphire_world::weather`, at most two states live, chosen by schedule, then region, then override, then a cutscene's key, then the tool. The schedule is a pure hash of the world clock, every rate is in world seconds (an accelerated clock accelerates weather), and ten presets ship: clear, overcast, drizzle, rain, storm, snow, blizzard, hail, sandstorm and mist. The look resolves in one order with a test: the zone's day, the weather state, atmosphere volumes, a cutscene, the tool. None of it enters the simulation. | Today's wind is a renderer struct (`crates/sapphire_render/src/frame.rs:654-680`) that springs and cloth never see (`crates/sapphire_anim/src/springs.rs:1801`), and five tracks each proposed a wind or a state of their own. Time of day resolves its base, then volumes, then a cutscene (`crates/sapphire_world/src/tod/time_of_day.rs:233-245`), and the stage puts the zone under the day under the tool (`crates/sapphire_stage/src/scene.rs:2691-2705`), so weather needs one stated place in that order. A hashed schedule needs no state in a save or a replay, and a later game could still move it into the simulation. | Weather in the simulation, chosen deliberately (the reopen line in "What not to do" stays); another order or precedence, said in W8a's row. |
| **The frame's uniforms** | `FrameUniforms.weather` carries wetness, puddles, snow and sand, filling its two spare lanes (`crates/sapphire_render/src/gpu_types.rs:93-95`); the frame's uniforms grow once, by one appended `cover` vec4 (ice, frost, the cover's kind and a glint scale, V5), and `time`'s spare lanes are never reused for weather. Y2 owns the five shares and their drying: wetness over 180 s, puddles 900, snow melting over 1,200, sand 1,800 and ice 900, re-warming over six game hours in 120 steps. | One growth re-proves the layout's tests once; a share in a spare lane moves no golden. | A second weather block, at a second layout change. |
| **One volume shape** | `ZoneShape { Sphere, Box { yaw }, Ellipsoid { yaw } }` in `sapphire_zone`, landed by whichever of V1 and W8a lands first, converting to `sapphire_world`'s `VolumeShape`, which gains `Ellipsoid` and a yaw on `Box`. Atmosphere and fog volumes, weather regions and wind volumes all place by it. | Four tracks each needed a placed shape; one type gives the editor one set of handles (T9b). | A shape per feature, each with its own handles. |
| **Foliage as a field** | Plants placed by rules cook to scatter sets of 16-byte instances with stable IDs per cell, culled by chunk on the CPU and drawn as one instanced draw a view, kind and level, each instance found by a search over `instance_index`, so no base vertex is needed. Trunks gain capsule colliders and the nav bake sees them (G1b), so only a zone with foliage colliders moves its digest. Grass is opaque blades casting no shadow on any tier (Desktop-High included), cut grass regrows over 20 s, and colour palettes are chosen at load, never blended. | A plant as an entity pays a 192-byte instance and a draw (`crates/sapphire_render/src/gpu_types.rs:220-244`): the hand-placed grove's 14 plants cost 1.05 ms of Desktop-High's GPU frame, about 75 µs a plant (spec §5.12), and view culling at 14,400 draws takes 207 to 255 µs, so a forest of 100,000 would spend 1.5 to 1.8 ms of CPU before it draws [E]. GPU-driven culling is rejected below, and the iOS Simulator lacks base vertex (`crates/sapphire_rhi/src/caps.rs:381-389`). Alpha-tested blades cost a tiler its hidden-surface removal, and grass shadows cost more than they show in this look; a player walking through trunks breaks it. | Plants stay entities (G2 drops), or trees get no colliders (G1b drops, every digest unchanged). |
| **Water beyond the lake** | One water family (W3's), its kinds by constants over a cooked info texture (bed depth, shore distance, flow). Oceans by at most four Gerstner waves and two detail normals, with a Rust twin within 1e-3 m that the swim's bob, floaters and shore events read; authored states keep waves under 0.35 m of amplitude [E]. Rivers flow on their own cooked polyline, never a camera spline. Diving is in; caustics are a generated pattern, with a dive pass on desktops only. The simulation sees no current. | A Gerstner sum is closed-form, the same on every tier and cheap to mirror on the CPU [U: GPU Gems' water chapter]; an FFT ocean needs compute and a spectral look the painted sea doesn't want. The camera splines sit outside the digest (`crates/sapphire_zone/src/cooked.rs:66-67`) and carry rail data. A current in the simulation would move replays for a look. | Still water only (H1 drops), or an FFT ocean, rejected below. |
| **Snow, sand and ice** | Cover is a variant of its own, `COVER_SURFACES`, never inside `WET_SURFACES` (`crates/sapphire_render/shaders/environment.wgsl:168`), with functions foliage (G4) and terrain (W13) share; ice is `ICE_SURFACES`, and a frozen body is the water body's kind with V6's look. Footprints are a `Stamp` decal shape in a pool of their own (V7, in the slice); trenches pressed into deep snow are V8a and V8b, a channel of W15b's buffer, on the desktops and Mobile-High. Tessellation stays out. | Wet zones shouldn't pay for snow; footprints are what a player sees first, and a decal costs no geometry; wgpu 30 has no tessellation. | Prints only (V8a and V8b drop). |
| **Atmosphere and exposure** | Atmosphere volumes placed by the zone with biome presets (V1); local fog volumes inside the existing fog ray (V2); eye adaptation within ±0.75 EV of the authored exposure, rising at 3 EV a second and falling at 1, authored only on Mobile-Low (V4). Darkness and carried lights are presentation only; a darkness that plays is a game's mechanic. Mobile-High's volumetric fog row is deleted (its flag is already false, `crates/sapphire_render/tiers.ron:279`), and froxel fog is V14a's measured package in the tail: if it fails its kill, `volumetric_fog` leaves `tiers.ron`. | The fog's density volumes are "later" today (`crates/sapphire_render/src/fog.rs:92-99`), exposure has no adaptation (`crates/sapphire_render/src/post.rs:287-288`), and the desktops' flag is read by nothing (`crates/sapphire_render/src/quality.rs:249-251`). A cave mouth reads wrong at one exposure. Froxels cost about 0.5 ms on a desktop [E] for what V2 and V10 may already give. | No adaptation (V4 drops); froxels by default on the desktops. |
| **Particles: two worlds and a stateless path** (reopening "What not to do"'s GPU particles row) | Combat keeps its `VfxWorld`; world effects (placements, ambience, weather's splashes and breath) get a second, owned by `OwnerId::WORLD` (N9), with rows of their own, so the world never spends combat's caps. Many cheap particles take a stateless GPU path: each particle a closed-form function of its seed and age, evaluated in the vertex stage by N8a's `procedural_particles.wgsl` and drawn as instanced quads, the dead ones collapsed, with no compute pass and no indirect draw, at 24,000, 16,000, 6,000 and 3,000 drawn a tier in place of `tiers.ron`'s unread 50,000 and 8,000 (`crates/sapphire_render/tiers.ron:184`, `:283`). Combat may play stateless emitters on its owner's clock, so hitstop holds them. Stateful compute particles are only N14's spike, on its trigger: Y's gust-curled storm or V's swirling motes failing by the maintainer's eye, or N10's counter, by the end of batch III. | The iOS Simulator has no indirect execution (`crates/sapphire_rhi/src/caps.rs:381-389`); a closed form is deterministic at any frame rate and keeps no buffer between frames; combat's CPU caps (512 particles on Mobile-Low, `crates/sapphire_vfx/src/budget.rs`) can't carry weather and ambience too. | CPU particles only (N8 drops, and W6 keeps positions of its own), or compute particles on N14's figure. |
| **Effects as an asset kind, and their atlas** | `Effects` becomes an asset kind and libraries cook (N1), under schema v5 with v1 to v4 read bit-identically. Painted sheets cook into one texture array per loaded library, 256-pixel layers, at most 32 MiB on the desktops and 8 MiB on the phones, resident whole and never streamed: the second narrow exception to "no texture arrays", beside terrain's. | Libraries load as documents today (`crates/sapphire_vfx/src/document.rs:354`, whose header still says schema 1 at `:1`); one array keeps an effects draw to one bind; an array resident whole never meets the streamer's texture replacement. | Sheets as separate textures, a bind each. |
| **Generated content, and no new crate** | Trees (four species at three ages, by space colonization [U: Runions, Lane and Prusinkiewicz, 2007]), ground cover, effect sheets, stamp and crack maps and every preset are generated by the pipeline. No licensed pack, no SpeedTree and no new crate: bevy_hanabi, bevy_water and bevy_feronia are read for technique only [U]. | The repository may ship no third-party tree or effect without m1-plan §3's provenance; SpeedTree's terms changed in 2025 [U: Unity's SpeedTree pages]; generated content proves cost and correctness, and the eye stays owed (§7, items 5 and 8). | An artist's or a licensed pack, with its provenance. |

### The cooker's rules

Every new cooked representation keeps these:

1. **A measured row first:** the row it acts on, a probe that prices it before the cooker work (often a run-time override that fakes the cooked result), and a kill criterion.
2. **A format version and a migration:** the importer's stage version so the content cache cooks it again (as `tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/character_maps/layer_mask.rs:29-31` does), the reader's document or asset version, and an old layout read for one release or refused with a clear error. Never a silent layout change.
3. **The golden policy, in the change:** which scenes move, exactly or by a re-baseline with a FLIP bound. A representation that claims exactness proves it with goldens that don't move.
4. **A cook-time budget per platform:** a `budget` cook is already minutes of the whole machine and about 1.1 GB of packs a cook ([m6-handoff.md](m6-handoff.md), "The benchmark"). A step adds under 10% to the cook, or caches by its input.
5. **The patch effect:** a format bump cooks every asset of its kind into the next patch, so until the pack cap (K2) lands a format change ships with a release, not a patch.
6. **A pipeline budget:** each `MaterialFlags` bit (`crates/sapphire_assets/src/material/mod.rs`) can multiply the family's pipeline keys (`crates/sapphire_assets/src/pipeline_key.rs`). A representation that reuses an existing branch is preferred to one that adds a permutation.
7. **Phone memory:** Mobile-Low peaks at 1,024 of its 1,638 MiB ([m5-exit-run.md](m5-exit-run.md), exit test 2). Anything resident counts its bytes on each phone tier.
8. **Determinism:** cooks are byte-identical across machines today (three machines cooked the same sets, [m5-plan.md](m5-plan.md), D0's row). A new step uses no thread that reorders output, no wall clock and no unseeded random number, and adds a byte-identity test.

### What not to do

Each idea moves a cost this engine doesn't pay, blurs the lines and band edges that make the look, or, from the production tracks on, spends people's time or a rule the engine lives by for less than something simpler. Each has what would reopen it.

| Idea | Why not | Reopen when |
|---|---|---|
| Variable-rate or half-rate shading, lines at a higher rate than fills | wgpu 30 has no variable-rate API; the two-tone split takes `fwidth`, so a 2×2 rate staircases it; the best published gains fall off at 1080p [U]. | A world-only half-rate prototype that holds line contrast. |
| Temporal reuse of lines | Moving lines keep no history (`temporal.rs:40-51`). | Never for the look. |
| The GPU-driven path: GPU culling and indirect draws, meshlets, mesh shaders, a visibility buffer, virtual geometry, micro-triangle clusters | `render` is about 1 ms at 2,000 to 4,000 calls [M, logs]; wgpu 30's Metal multi-draw is a CPU loop; the `lods` pair shows triangle cuts convert poorly into time on this GPU. D11 was cut for the same reason ([m6-handoff.md](m6-handoff.md), "Cut from M5"). | `render` over a few ms on a phone at shipping counts. |
| A render thread, one packet ahead | The CPU's p95 is at most 3.0 ms against 8 to 22 ms lines; the queue of two already overlaps CPU and GPU; a render thread adds a frame of latency. | B7 shows a tier CPU-limited with overlap, or a phone's CPU p95 over half its interval. |
| Texture arrays or bindless banks, a world material table, retained draw records | At most about 0.3 ms of CPU; arrays fix one size and level count, while the streamer replaces texture objects (`crates/sapphire_render/src/materials.rs:563-565`). Two narrow exceptions, each fixed in size per cook, resident whole and never streamed: terrain's layer array (W12, §3's "Terrain inside zones") and the effects atlas (N4b, §3's "Effects as an asset kind"). | A trace blaming bind or rebuild time (J6). |
| Baked shadows, contact bakes, a static and dynamic shadow layer split | The measured invalidator is the light step, which would clear a static layer too; movers redraw a cached cascade on 0 to 2.2% of frames ([m6-handoff.md](m6-handoff.md), "Draws and levels"). | Zones with windy foliage or skinned movers inside cached cascades. |
| Screen-space outlines in place of hulls | A pass over every pixel every frame, losing authored width and per-region colour. Hulls stay; their program is cut (P2). | Never for the look. |
| Skipping or cropping the character prepass in play | It is at most about 0.2 ms mean on Desktop-High and 31 to 71 µs on Mobile-Low [M, logs], the player is nearly always in view, and Apple compresses cleared regions. | A tiler's external-bytes counter (X3). |
| Deformation blocks, projected-error animation scheduling, clip codecs, SoA animation, GPU crowds | GPU skinning is about 1% of the frame, animation and IK 0.03 to 0.05 ms, the crowd of sixty about 0.05 ms ([m5-exit-run.md](m5-exit-run.md), exit tests 5 and 6). | B8's counter over 0.2 ms; crowds in the hundreds. |
| Whole-chain or headroom texture raises | +1.37 GB on desktop [E]; defeats streaming on phones. | Never as a default. |
| A native tile-memory backend or a wgpu fork | No pair of same-pixel passes exists that wgpu can't merge: bloom, the upscale, temporal AA, GTAO and the character readers all need neighbours. | A device showing a round trip dominating. |
| Stateful GPU particles kept in compute, order-independent transparency, half-resolution effects on phones, cooked fill budgets | Stateless GPU particles are allowed (N8; §3, "Particles: two worlds and a stateless path"); state kept in compute needs buffers across frames and, to draw only the living, indirect draws the iOS Simulator lacks, while effects' CPU simulation costs 20 to 30 µs at a duel's worst. Order-independent transparency needs an extra target and a composite and mutes overlapping layers [U: McGuire and Bavoil, 2013], while anime effects are mostly additive and each effect already sorts back to front. Mobile-Low has no depth for half-resolution effects to composite against, and half resolution blurs the hard edges that are the look. A cooked pixel estimate is wrong once the camera or the tier changes; N10's run-time estimate and N12's gauge serve instead. | Compute: N14's trigger. Order-independent transparency: an authored pack whose covering layers visibly interpenetrate in a capture. Half resolution: N10 showing large soft world sprites over a stated share of Mobile-High's frame, and then only for emitters marked `soft_volume`, on Mobile-High. Cooked fill budgets: B5 or N10 showing fill as the cost, cutout geometry first ("Waiting on a trigger"). |
| Rate-distortion BC7 or supercompressed ASTC as frame-time work | They change download size, not frame time. | A download-size goal of its own. |
| Per-cell texture atlases | The district's sets are shared across cells, so atlases copy them several times into phone memory ([m6-handoff.md](m6-handoff.md), "Draws and levels"). | Content whose cells own their sets. |
| Profile-guided builds, BOLT, frame arenas | No CPU row is near a line, and fat LTO is already on. | A phone CPU row near its line. |
| Neural upscaling or textures, ray-traced or dynamic GI, lightmaps, a deferred phone path | No measured cost on a mid-range phone; photoreal tools against an authored look. | A vendor's measurement under about 2 ms on such a phone. |
| The running game inside the editor's process or window | A crash or a stalled frame in the game would take the editor and its unsaved documents down; Godot embeds a separate process, not a shared one [U: Godot's documentation]. | Never. |
| Another editor toolkit, or Bevy's or Fyrox's editor | It throws away 344 tests and the 0-pixel viewport route; Bevy's editor is prototypes [U]. | T12's pass row missed by egui's layout, not the engine's code. |
| Undo as whole-document snapshots, per-pane stacks or one global stack | Per-document command logs with set steps across them are what Godot's and Blender's designs settled on [U: their documentation]; one global stack undoes closed documents [U: The Machinery's 2018 post]. | Never. |
| Editor data (layout, colours, comments) in runtime documents | It recooks a document on a drag and puts editor types in a runtime crate. | Never. |
| A tools crate as a dependency of anything | Nothing may depend on a tool (`xtask/layers.toml:11`); terrain edits live in `sapphire_zone`. | Never. |
| Live multi-user editing, or one file per placement | A server and conflict rules for a team of one to five; thousands of files and partial submits [U: Epic's documentation]. Layer files and a merge driver give most of the value. | A team of over about ten on one zone, or a studio asking for live co-editing. |
| A material node graph, or gameplay visual scripting | The look is authored by anime material parameters and ramps, and graphs invite a pipeline per material; Luau is the gameplay language. | A look the region model can't express. |
