| Artists' sources, cooked outputs, caches or thumbnails in this repository | Goldens are already 86% of the history; derived data belongs in a cache, and bytes that vary by GPU churn. | Never. |
| Importing `.blend` in the cook; Maya or Max plugins or the Autodesk FBX SDK; checks written twice | Blender in every key and a GPL program in the cook path; proprietary SDKs, where ufbx already reads FBX; two definitions of a triangle drift. | A pinned Blender in CI and a team that refuses an export step. |
| A general asset database, a server-side importer, a pipeline daemon or a cook farm | Content hashes and C2's index do the first two's work; `content --watch` closes the loop; a deterministic cook with a shared cache gets most of a farm [E]. | T5 measuring process start over 30% of a cook (a daemon); cold cooks a warm cache can't bring under a few minutes (a farm). |
| A shared cache without the architecture in a texture's key, or writable by anyone | It would serve a Mac's texture blocks to a Linux cook, and a forgotten stage bump would poison every machine (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/cache.rs:16-21`). | Never. |
| Hair cards or strand hair | The look wants opaque modelled shells with a painted highlight; alpha-tested hair costs coverage no character pays today. | The coverage cook's trigger (below). |
| FFT or simulated oceans, tessellated water, physical caustics, volumetric clouds, a physically based sky | Physical and per-frame compute for a painted look of bands, foam and colour; wgpu 30 has no tessellation. Allowed instead: at most four Gerstner waves with a CPU twin (H1a), generated caustics (H4), and snow's footprints and trenches without tessellation (V7, V8a, V8b). | A game whose sea is the stage. |
| Water depth from the scene prepass alone | Mobile-Low never has one, and Mobile-High only while GTAO runs, which F3 may remove. | Never as the only source. |
| Particles for falling weather, or a shelter depth drawn at run time | W6's streaks already take their positions from the stateless include (N8a) with no state to keep, and Y5's curl and far sheets bend them in gusts; CPU particles would starve combat's caps (512 on Mobile-Low, `crates/sapphire_vfx/src/budget.rs`); roofs are static. | A blizzard or sandstorm arm whose flakes show visible repeat or sheet slide by the maintainer's eye, with N's stateless path landed and the arm within 1.5× W6's row; destructible roofs. |
| Planar reflections by a second scene render, SSR on phones, stochastic glossy SSR | A second render doubles draws; Mobile-Low has no depth, and Genshin Impact's own talk called its SSR too dear for mobile [U: a 2022 write-up of a 2020 talk]; this look's puddles are mirror-flat. | A device showing Mobile-High with over 2 ms of headroom. |
| Weather, wind, waves, cover, effects, grass bending or wetness that the simulation reads | Presentation never moves a checksum; a move doused by rain reads a story flag; replays and combat would read presentation, and the digests prove they don't. | A design that needs weather in the simulation, chosen deliberately (W8a's hashed schedule could move there). |
| Terrain virtual texturing, world partition, GPU-driven grass or foliage, compute or indirect draws as any feature's main path | Zones are at most 4 km and arrive behind wipes; CPU placement per cell is deterministic and costs nothing a frame; the iOS Simulator has neither indirect execution nor base vertex (`crates/sapphire_rhi/src/caps.rs:381-389`), so G2 culls on the CPU and N8b collapses dead particles in the vertex stage. | An open world. |
| A second wind, weather state, volume shape, stateless particle include or ring include | Every track reads Y1, W8a, `ZoneShape`, N8a's include and the one ring include; a private copy drifts and doubles a golden's causes. | Never. |
| A uniform branch where a pipeline constant will do: cover, ice, the storm sky, foliage's weather | L4's uniform branch moved six goldens ([m6-handoff.md](m6-handoff.md), "Known limits and risks"); each is a variant or a constant. | Never. |
| Snow in `WET_SURFACES`; a post variant for under water | Wet zones would pay for snow, so `COVER_SURFACES` is a variant of its own; an under-water post variant doubles post's combinations, and the fog uniforms draw it (W4). | Never. |
| World effects on combat's caps; the zone's camera splines as rivers | Combat's caps are the fight's: ring events are analytic and world effects have their own `VfxWorld` and rows. The splines sit outside the digest and carry rail data (`crates/sapphire_zone/src/cooked.rs:66-67`). | Never. |
| A new CPU rasterizer for impostors | The cooker's ray caster already bakes cut-out, two-sided geometry. | Never. |
| Alpha-tested grass, grass shadows by default, blended palettes, outlined foliage | A tiler loses hidden-surface removal under alpha testing; grass shadows cost more than they show; the look wants clean colour regions, and lines on characters. | A look the maintainer asks for, within the grass row. |
| Froxel fog by default, or `volumetric_fog` left on with nothing reading it | Froxels cost about 0.5 ms on a desktop for what fog volumes and beams may give; a flag nobody reads promises a feature. | V14a's verdict. |
| Growing `FrameUniforms` more than once, or weather in `time`'s spare lanes | Each growth re-proves the layout; `time`'s lanes are time's. | Never. |
| W1 waiting on a content generator | W1 reserves an area for each track, and each fills its own in its own pull request. | Never. |
| Third-party foliage, water, effects or sky assets downloaded or committed; GPL code copied | m1-plan §3's provenance rule; generated content proves cost and correctness. | Content with provenance, through §7. |
| Root motion from locomotion, physics-driven characters, or Rapier or Avian in the simulation | Every graph edit would move every replay, and rollback-safe animation cost one studio about two engineer-years [U: a GDC 2018 talk]; contact determinism across architectures and allocation-free steps for no gameplay gain. | Never for this engine's fights. |
| Run-time retargeting, bone-driven faces or facial capture, run-time lip sync, full-body IK, "climb anything", wall-runs, mounts | A second path move tables can't share; bones swim the SDF face shadow and realism is the wrong target; cook-time visemes are deterministic and free; two-bone chains already place hands and feet; each of the rest is a subsystem a zone game doesn't need. | A design that asks for one. |
| LAFAN1 or ripped game motion | Licence and copyright. | Never. |
| Middleware, HRTF or ambisonics, convolution reverb, Opus, a DSP node graph, rooms and portals with diffraction | Accounts and native builds on five platforms; players hear this on phone speakers and TVs; cost scales with sources and impulse responses need provenance; pure-Rust Opus decoders are young [U]; designers need containers, a mix and a profiler first. | A private game behind `AudioBackend`; a headphones mode measured on a phone; a listener saying kira's reverb can't make a space; interiors joined by doorways. |
| Allocating or decoding on the audio thread; audio feeding the simulation | The mix must not glitch, and the simulation never waits on audio. | Never. |

### Waiting on a trigger

These are designed and would act on a measured row, but the row isn't here yet.

| Item | Waiting for | Then |
|---|---|---|
| **The coverage cook** for cut-out hair, lashes or cloth: an opaque interior and an alpha-tested fringe, with an alpha-coverage-preserving mip filter on the fringe's map (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/texture/mips.rs` averages alpha today); no new pipeline | Content with `Mask` materials: today content holds 77 opaque material documents, 5 blended and none masked, and no character model declares a mask (K1's census) | A package, killed under 0.1 ms of Desktop-High's `gpu main` |
| **ASTC block size by class,** and **a slimmer phone colour target** (the character mask in an `R8Unorm` target of its own, `Rg11b10Ufloat` colour beside it) | A phone session: every mobile class defaults to 4×4 (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/texture.rs:287`), and the colour target's gain is a tile-storage question only a device's counters answer | Packages sized by X3's readings |

| **FBX parity for anime data** (C13): outline normals, material IDs and second UV sets from FBX | A Maya or 3ds Max artist | The package |
| **Authored character levels** (C14) | K1's audit showing the simplifier over its error on an authored character, or a simplified face that breaks | The package |
| **The shared cook cache and its hygiene** (C10, C11) | A host (§7) | The packages; exit test 10's second half |
| **Content roots and the repository split** (C15) | An artist joining, and §3's version-control choice made | The package |
| **A long-lived pipeline server for the editor** | T5 measuring process start over 30% of a cook | A package |
| **A reverb of the engine's own** (a feedback delay network taking the same presets) | A listener judging kira's reverb short of a space | A package |
| **Grapples and swimming** (A18, A19) | A16b landing (§3, "Traversal, and how far") | The packages |
| **The effects library split into one file per effect** | Over about 200 entries, or frequent conflicts on one entry | A package |
| **Device-only items:** the phones' frame fit with dynamic resolution; the presented lane and press-to-glass; a discrete GPU (the depth-equal pass, overdraw, D3D12's ignored discards); an M1 to M4 Mac (fragment temporal AA with compressible targets, since only the M5 compresses storage textures among Apple's Macs); Android pre-rotation (wgpu hard-codes an identity pre-transform); temporal reconstruction on Mobile-High; cropping the character prepass on a tiler; MSAA depth resolve, if a phone shows Mobile-High's scene prepass over 10% of its frame | The devices (§7), with X3's command for the phones | Packages sized by what each device shows |

### What wgpu 30 allows

Read from wgpu 30.0.1's source and changelog [U]: memoryless transient attachments on Metal and Vulkan (a no-op on D3D12), in-pass MSAA resolve, a discarding store and an undefined load, `SHADER_F16` on all three backends (requested by the engine, used by no shader), subgroups, immediates, dual-source blending, render bundles, and timestamp queries inside passes, though not on Apple GPUs, which time only at pass boundaries. Every package in §4 fits inside that: the interface into the last render pass, effects into a second resolve output, the formats and the half-float spike are portable.

It doesn't allow subpasses, input attachments or framebuffer fetch; Metal tile shaders or programmable blending; variable-rate shading; **MSAA depth resolve,** the one gap with a measured price, since it is why Mobile-High draws a scene prepass for GTAO; Metal heaps, residency sets or sparse textures; a working Metal pipeline cache; Metal multi-draw on the GPU; D3D12 discard; Android pre-rotation; or presentation timing outside `VK_GOOGLE_display_timing`. M6 takes 30.0.2 (X1) and waits on upstream for depth resolve, local reads and `TextureDescriptor::theoretical_memory_footprint`. The production tracks' GPU work fits too: storage buffers, `R32Uint` loads, and `R16Unorm` heights where the adapter has 16-bit norm formats (requested at `crates/sapphire_rhi/src/caps.rs:38`), `R16Uint` with a manual filter where it doesn't; MetalFX and DLSS run their own passes outside wgpu's [U].

### From m6-handoff's "What M6 inherits from the exit run"

- **Desktop-High's exit test 1, the units with no valid round, which prepasses row exit test 5 judges:** B0.
- **The report's tables reading void rounds' GPU rows:** B1.
- **Not run again at the commit measured** (the ten recipes, `zero_hitches` and the district fight cold and warm on Metal, the sandbox's replays tests on Metal): Z1's rehearsal and Z2, and exit test 7.
- **The lock's floor on short units:** B1 and §3's "Void rounds".
- **The device command's lost launch and the crowd's cell edge:** X4.

### From m6-handoff's "Everything deferred"

- **Taken into M6:**
  - the streamer's CPU on Desktop-Low, in the handoff's order (J4), and the raise that slows every pass it overlaps on Metal (J5);
  - packs with no size cap (K2), and the game ignoring the low-memory event (X2);
  - `Combat::restart` in the game's frame (J2);
  - the depth-equal main pass built and off, and the desktops' opaque surfaces drawn a call a draw (F5, chosen per GPU family on its probe, D10's stencil fix with it);
  - cooked levels that save little, and the heroine's vertices by level (K1's report, K3);
  - the screen effects' share of post's row, measured with them on (B5);
  - the figure generator's control maps that bleed at coarse levels, which K4 removes on the figures it folds;
  - the probe keys' memory, the streamed keys waiting on their trigger (above);
  - by the production tracks: the editor's build-out and artist tools (T, C); a cook-time skeleton cut for an authored character (C8); motion intake from FBX packs and reactions beyond what exists (A2, A8); screen-space reflections and a water plane (W10, W3); ragdolls (A10, A11); DLSS (W17c, on §7's yes); audio beyond voice in conversations (U); terrain and traversal inside zones (W12 to W15, A14 to A20); a zone's cutscene that frames no fighter in the arena's space (T16b); a line spoken only on a published cue (U4's events);
  - by the later round's tracks: the live GPU particles row (spec §3.4), restated as stateless particles drawn (N0) and enforced by N10's budgets.
- **Waiting, with the reason:**
  - **The simulation's data from packs, a save across zones, a run-wide `tables_hash`, the script VM's heap in replays and per-script boards:** for the milestone that builds game systems.
  - **A golden re-capture pass for Metal, and a figure generator that pads its cells:** no gate needs them; they are churn for no frame time.
  - **GPU buffers that only grow and the job system's allocation per parallel call:** no measured row; J6's spans would show them.
  - **A tier change rebuilding the renderer twice around a trip, held spot tiles in the texture estimate, "redraws only what moved" in a cached cascade:** none acts on a measured frame row.
  - **The phones' active enemies row** (six against the benchmark's twelve): a budget question for the maintainer; the over-read is stated in §1.
  - **Graph v2's cap on clips sampled** (§3's 8): the heroine's converted graph samples 10 an evaluation of the layers, so the compiler's cap is 12 an evaluation (set at A5a's review); her worst frame by v1's code is 191 clips (a landing's hand-off, which starts a decay), 345 with a turn's hand-off on the same frame. A5b decides whether v2 keeps v1's phase matching by whole evaluations; the maintainer settles the cap.
  - **Gate 5 with no nightly device run, dynamic resolution off by default:** the maintainer's (§7 asks the last). The GPU particles row is taken (above).
  - Everything else in the handoff's "Everything deferred", unchanged.

### Spec §11's open questions

M6 answers none of them. Q4 (Windows' default backend), Q5's phone measurements, Q9 (device floors, throttled) and Q14 (Android pre-rotation) still need devices; X3 makes their phone readings one command.

**Not in M6:** game systems (stats, levels, equipment, inventory, elements, quests, a map, interactables) and the simulation's data from packs; a seamless open world, world partition and terrain streaming beyond a zone; a sea as the stage (sailing), and weather, currents or darkness as game mechanics (the reopen lines are in "What not to do"); the interface's motion design, text entry and more languages; accounts, online services and telemetry; co-op; ray tracing; consoles. Every device check [m6-handoff.md](m6-handoff.md) lists stays owed.

## 4. Work packages

Each package is one pull request into `main`, or a few. Sizes are as §3 says. Each finished package gets an "As built" note in its row and in its spec §5 section; a killed one gets "As built: killed", its measurement and why.

The usual rules carry over:

- Existing goldens stay byte-identical unless a row says otherwise, and an old scene pins a new feature off.
- A new scene commits only a Metal golden.
- A feature builds its pipelines only when its work can run, declares them to the inventory, and joins the warm-up and `declares_every_setting`.
- Every system publishes a stat counter (R9) against its budget.
- No checksum moves unless a row says so.
- Each new document struct denies unknown fields.
- A cook step stays on the deterministic maths, so cooks agree across architectures.
- A rule from [m6-handoff.md](m6-handoff.md)'s "Rules the reviews made" binds whatever it touches.
- A look package's row names its four tiers: Desktop-High, Desktop-Low, Mobile-High and Mobile-Low.

M6's own, for every row below:

- **Measure first** names the probe that prices the package before it is built: a B9 run, a unit B4 to B8 adds, or a B6 switch. **Kill if** names the criterion that stops it. **Done when** is what lands it.
- Gains are ranges with their marks. A package's gain is judged on `gpu frame` p95 from valid rounds, alternated against its base in one sitting.
- Agents prove a package with counts, tests and goldens, and hand over the timed script; the orchestrator runs every timed figure, in a quiet window.

The renderer has no `dev` feature, so its half of each switch is a hidden settings field, and only the sandbox's `dev` flags reach it. Counters: character triangles by pass, character pixels and hull pixels from the ID target, and wholly flat 32-wide groups in the phones' edge upscaler (F1's). **Measure first:** none. **Kill if:** never. **Done when:** each switch is a sandbox flag a B9 arm names; a test holds every tier's shipping inventory, keys and count, unchanged; and every golden is byte-identical with the switches at their defaults. | M | Plan | **As built (M6 B6):** `sapphire_render::measure` and `shaders/measure.wgsl`. Eleven `MEASURE_*` override constants in `anime_character.wgsl` (outline hulls read them through `outline.wgsl`), each the shipping value by default and passed to the anime character family's main-pass pipelines only when a switch is on: `--measure-no-rim`, `--measure-no-local-lights`, `--measure-light-cap <n>` (a cap in the shader, characters alone, since the tiers have none), `--measure-one-received-slot`, `--measure-no-hull-shadow`, `--measure-unlit-hulls`, `--measure-one-ramp-row`, `--measure-material-read two-levels|fractional|whole`, `--measure-one-hair-style` (P3's uniform branch on the draw's style), `--measure-face-light-constant` (a fixed head-space light, both face-map taps kept) and `--measure-control-unbound` (every character, so it stands for K4 only where the control maps are constant per region, as the generated figures' are). The renderer's half is `RenderSettings::character_switches`, hidden; tiers set the default, and the sandbox refuses the flags without `dev`. Counters: `PassCounts::character_triangles` in every build, and, under `--measure-pixels` (`RenderSettings::measure_pixels`), two compute passes counting the main view's character pixels (scene colour's character mask), the hull pixels among them (no character ID in the character prepass's target, so a line over another character goes uncounted) and the edge upscaler's 8 × 4 groups and the wholly flat ones, read back a few frames late into `RenderStats::measured`; the bench's new group "the character pixel" logs all eight rows. Proved: every golden byte-identical with the switches at their defaults (458 of 463 scenes identical in one full Metal run at head against the base's; the other 5 two to seven pixels apart by at most two code values, four of them identical captured again alone, and `game-hud-party-ready-ja` three pixels apart by one code value both times); `measure::tests` (no tier sets a switch, the default passes no constant, each switch its one, both programs declare each at its shipping value); `inventory::the_measurement_switches_build_every_tiers_inventory_and_nothing_more` (on every tier's settings every switch on builds the shipping renderer's pipelines from a cook's keys, its uploads nothing more, and the counters exactly their two); the sandbox's flag test; and a counted walk of the party fight with each flag (`--bench 140 --draw-every 7`, Desktop-High at 1280 × 720: 83,366 main character triangles, about 25,300 character pixels and 1,600 hull pixels; Mobile-High at 2532 × 1170: 92,881 upscale groups, 4,540 of them wholly flat). The district's counts table gains the eight rows when it is next refreshed; B9's arms name the flags. |
| B7 | **Overlap and a phone-shaped CPU.** The bench waits for the GPU every frame (`apps/sapphire_sandbox/src/bench.rs:413`), so nothing says whether a tier is CPU-limited when CPU and GPU overlap, as a shipped frame does. An offscreen overlap mode waits on submission N−2, not N, and logs the queue's age on the GPU's clock. The phone units gain `--compute-threads 1\|3` arms beside the desktop's nine workers. Untested whether wgpu 30's poll by submission index behaves alike on the three backends [U]. It decides the render thread's trigger (§3, "What not to do"). **Measure first:** none. **Kill if:** never. **Done when:** an overlap run on each tier reads CPU- or GPU-limited, and the phone units have their pool-shaped arms in B9. | S | B3 | **As built (M6 B7):** `--bench-overlap` (off by default, a timed bench alone: not with `--draw-every` or `--serial-passes`) makes a plain bench and a fight's bench wait after frame N's submit for the frame graph's submission of N−2 by index (`apps/sapphire_sandbox/src/bench/overlap.rs`), each phase starting from a drained queue. Each GPU phase logs `bench overlap`: `limited=gpu` where more than half its frames' CPU waited 100 µs or longer for the frame two back, `cpu` otherwise (read on the CPU's clock, needing no lining up); on the GPU's clock, how much work was queued ahead of a frame at its submit (`behind_us_*`), the queue's age from submit to GPU end (`queue_age_us_*`) and the GPU's frame interval. The two clocks are lined up by the waits' returns (a wait for N−2 returns once that frame has ended on the GPU, never before, so the most of those bounds holds against a return read late), or, with fewer than eight frames that waited, by the median lag from a submit to its frame's start over the frames that met an idle GPU (`lined_up_by`). The waits by index are held by the renderer's GPU test `graph::waiting_on_a_submission_two_frames_back_reads_its_timings`: run on Metal; Vulkan (lavapipe) and Direct3D 12 (WARP) only where CI runs it. Under overlap a phase reads every frame's timings that came back (`GraphExecutor::gpu_timings_read`), since one wait can bring back two; only `cpu frame`, `gpu wait` and `bench overlap` compare across the arms, as overlapped frames' GPU work can overlap. `bench-scale report` prints `bench overlap` under its run. `bench/scale.ron` gains `fight-pool` (the district fight on Mobile-High and Mobile-Low with arms `one` and `three`, `--compute-threads 1` and `3`, beside `mac`, this Mac's own pool) and `fight-overlap` (every tier, `waits` against `overlaps`); `bench-scale plan` lays them out. ### Track S: shadows (exit test 2)

| ID | Package | Size | Depends on | As built |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| S1 | **Latch the shadow light.** `ShadowPlan` takes the quantized light direction anew whenever `fit::quantize` steps (`crates/sapphire_render/src/shadows/plan.rs:267-275`; the step, `crates/sapphire_render/src/shadows/fit.rs:27-29`), and every cached cascade is drawn again. With the hour running, light steps fall on 966 of the exit walk's 7,600 frames, 12.7% [M, logs]. Accept a new direction at most every N frames or past an angle, and keep counting `light_steps`. The key light moves on an authored schedule, and a hard two-tone shadow edge carries a small step better than a soft penumbra: about 12 cm at a 3 m post's tip at dusk [E]. It acts on Desktop-High's `exit-walk`, GPU frame p95 11.80 ms (the runbook), its shadows 3.70 ms at p95 against 2.14 with the hour held; Desktop-Low's shadows 2.55 against 1.39; Mobile-High's 1.10 against 0.25 [M, logs]. **Gain:** up to 1.4 ms at p95 on Desktop-High (12%), up to 1.0 on Desktop-Low, up to 0.2 on Mobile-High, about none on Mobile-Low, nothing at p50 [M as a bound, E as realized]. Four tiers: the latch on all four, its N or angle a tier setting. **Measure first:** `running-hour`. **Kill if:** the running against held gap in Desktop-High's GPU frame p95 is under 0.2 ms; under 0.5 ms, the latch lands and S2 is killed. **Done when:** Desktop-High's `running-hour` shadows p95 is within 0.3 ms of held on a valid round; a test holds the step's size and how often it is taken; goldens byte-identical, or any scene that runs the hour named and re-baselined with its FLIP bound. | S | B9 | |
| S2 | **Per-cascade light spaces, or a spare layer, if S1 isn't enough.** A light basis per cascade in `ShadowCascade` (`crates/sapphire_render/shaders/frame.wgsl:816-822`, `:858-873`), with coarser quanta for cached cascades, and a caster level setting per cascade in place of the shared `CASCADE_TEXELS` (`crates/sapphire_render/src/levels.rs:23-30`). It acts on what S1 leaves of the 1.4 ms. **Measure first:** `running-hour` after S1. **Kill if:** the residual gap is under 0.3 ms. **Done when:** the residual is under 0.3 ms, the `shadows-*` goldens are byte-identical or within their gates, and no acne shows in the shadow tests' scenes. | M | S1 | |

### Track P: the anime character pixel (exit tests 2, 3 and 6)

The character pixel is where Desktop-High misses. In the arena's `party-kinds` on Desktop-High 15.1% of the pixels are character pixels, 28% of those hull lines; in the district fight at tick 801, 18.8%, 23% hull lines. Opaque overdraw is 2.04 fragments on the mean character pixel, most hidden by Apple's hidden-surface removal except where dithered fades and alpha tests force late depth [M, logs, counted]. P1 to P4 and K4 change WGSL that includes each other's files, so they land one at a time (§5).

| ID | Package | Size | Depends on | As built |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | **Bound the received-character-shadow slots.** `received_visibility` (`crates/sapphire_render/shaders/anime_character.wgsl:497-523`) loops over the active slots the CPU sets (`characters[c].shadow.received`), each a 9-tap lookup in another character's map: four slots on Desktop-High, two on Desktop-Low. A cap a tier (two on Desktop-High), the characters past it taking the phones' capsule term (about 40 operations, no reads), and the hull none (P2). Another character's shadow is a soft darkening the two-tone split already blurs. It acts on Desktop-High `district-fight`'s main pass, 5.81 / 6.65 ms p50 / p95, 52% of its frame [M, logs]; L12 measured the received maps at 0.18 ms on Desktop-High with three characters at 1080p and 0.33 on Desktop-Low (spec §5.12, "As built (M5 L12)"). **Gain:** 0.1 to 0.3 ms on Desktop-High at the busiest frames, up to 0.15 on Desktop-Low, none on the phones, which already take capsules [E]. Four tiers: Desktop-High capped at two; Desktop-Low at its two; the phones unchanged. **Measure first:** `df-terms` and `pk-terms` with other characters' shadows off bound it; B6's one-slot switch prices slots two to four. **Kill if:** other characters' shadows off lowers Desktop-High `district-fight`'s `gpu main` p50 by under 0.1 ms; if one slot moves it by under 0.05 ms, Desktop-High keeps four. **Done when:** the district fight's main pass p95 falls by at least 0.1 ms, and L12's scenes are re-baselined within the row's stated FLIP bound with the look gate passing. | S | B9 | |
| P2 | **One shadow path for hull pixels.** `outline_pixel` computes both `cast_shadow_at` (9 to 18 taps) and `received_visibility` (0 to 36) and keeps one (`crates/sapphire_render/shaders/outline.wgsl:249-252`). In their place, one hardware-filtered compare read of the cascade (2×2 filtering in a single `textureSampleCompareLevel`), the per-character `face_shadow` on faces, and no received loop: a dark line 1 to 5 px wide hides any penumbra. Hull pixels are 23 to 28% of character pixels (157,669 at the district fight's tick 801 on Desktop-High, counted on the `ci` cook) and every shipped material lights its line [M, logs]. Computing both and selecting came by analogy to the WARP fix for an early return in non-uniform flow (`1aff105c9`, then `a2e6c1a94`), so the dx12-warp golden job must compile the rewrite. **Gain:** 0.05 to 0.1 ms on Desktop-High; the hulls in all are 0.2 to 0.35 ms at the busiest frame [E]; a shorter hull program may also use fewer registers [U]. Four tiers: the same read on all four, replacing the phones' 4-tap tent too. **Measure first:** outlines off in `pk-terms` and `df-terms` (hull pixels and geometry together), and B6's hull-shadow-skipped switch (the chain). **Kill if:** skipping the hull's shadow moves Desktop-High `district-fight`'s `gpu main` by under 0.03 ms; it then lands as cleanup with P3. If outlines off moves it by over 0.5 ms, B6's hulls-unlit switch is priced next. **Done when:** the switch's measured share is recovered at a passing look gate, and the goldens where a lit line crosses a shadow edge are re-baselined with the row's FLIP bound. | S | B9, P1 | |
| P3 | **Exact cleanups of the character shader, in one bundle.** Face light per face on the CPU: `HeadLight::new` (`crates/sapphire_assets/src/character_maps.rs:279-318`) into `CharacterData`, in place of `face_margin`'s per-pixel `atan2`, `asin` and `cross` (`anime_character.wgsl:538-575`); a branch on the material's hair style before the one `fwidth`, in place of three highlight coordinates and a select (`:1260-1281`; WGSL's uniformity allows a branch on uniform data); one ramp row at full night (`:723-726`), and its comment; and, optionally, a compact per-draw block of the dozen material fields every pixel reads. Face light is per character and the hair style per material by construction. **Gain:** each well under 0.05 ms, and not additive: removing paths one at a time recovered nothing in M1's measurement, though dead layer paths once cost 18% of a one-character Mobile-Low main pass through register allocation (spec §5.12) [E]. Four tiers alike. **Measure first:** B6's ramp, hair and face switches together against base on `party-kinds`, and the offline compilers' register counts (§7). **Kill if:** the bundle moves `gpu main` by under 0.05 ms and the registers don't fall; then it lands only as cleanup when P2 or P4 opens the file. **Done when:** goldens byte-identical but for faces and night scenes: the CPU's maths differs from the GPU's fast `atan2` in low bits, so every face golden is proved again and any that moves is re-baselined with the row's FLIP bound. | S | B9, P2 | |
| P4 | **One whole-level read for flat material maps.** `material_sample` reads two whole levels and blends them, so streaming stays exact (`crates/sapphire_render/shaders/materials.wgsl:365-376`): one read at the fractional level moved a lowered texture's pixels by a code value or two on the M5, and at whole levels a lowered texture and a whole one read alike. One read at a whole level (`round(level)`, or `floor(level + bias)`) keeps that exactness. It applies to control maps (already nearest for their ID) and flat base colour; the face map and any map with painted lines keep two reads, since lines are what trilinear filtering exists for, and a snapped level steps and shimmers on a moving figure. It acts on every character pixel: 3 of about 17 reads and about 30 of 390 operations [counted from the shader]. Not additive with K4. **Gain:** 0.2 to 0.3 ms of Desktop-High's main pass at the busiest frames, about 0.05 on phones [E]. Four tiers alike. **Measure first:** B6's one-read switch, at the fractional level (hardware trilinear) and at a whole level, with a temporal look check on moving figures. **Kill if:** Desktop-High `district-fight`'s `gpu main` falls by under 0.1 ms. **Done when:** the gain holds; every character golden is re-baselined by decision (a code value or two) with the row's FLIP bound; new tests hold exactness at whole levels across a lowered texture; and a person's eye on a moving figure is owed. | M | B9, P3 | |
| P5 | **Character levels chosen by pixels per triangle.** `view_cull::choose_character_levels` takes the coarsest level within one output pixel of error at the sphere's nearest point (`crates/sapphire_render/src/levels.rs:63`, `crates/sapphire_render/src/view_cull.rs:386-392`). A second test: the coarsest level whose front triangles cover at least k pixels on average (k about 4 to 8; Arm's guidance asks 10 to 20 [U]), from K3's cooked constants. The pinned set (`game/game_combat/src/fight/posing.rs:179-185`) keeps the hero and close-ups whole, and D5's gates hold line width and the band's spread at every switch. It acts on quad efficiency: about 2.6 pixels a front triangle in `party-kinds` on Desktop-High, about 0.5 to 0.7 on the phones under 4× MSAA [E]; about 4 of the district fight's 14 figures already draw above level 0 on desktop. **Gain:** 0 to about 1 ms on Desktop-High [E, low confidence]; perhaps more on phones. Four tiers: k a tier setting. **Measure first:** `pk-lods` and `df-terms`' character-level arms, read with "skinned instances above level 0". **Kill if:** Desktop-High's `gpu main` falls by no more than the skinned-vertex share predicts (under 0.2 ms with ten or more figures coarsened); on the phones, if `gpu frame` moves by under 5%. **Done when:** the gain holds, `render::level_switches` passes at every new switch, and no pinned character coarsens. | M | K3, B9 | |
| P6 | **Local lights on characters: price, then a rig fill.** B6's light cap swept at 64, 8 and 0 on the night tour. In the measured fight 99.3% of character pixels walk an empty light list (counted on the `ci` cook) [M, logs], so this is for night town scenes. If the loop matters, a character's lesser lights fold into one rig fill on the CPU (`crates/sapphire_look/src/light.rs:104-128`). Four tiers: the phones' 8 and Mobile-Low's lights unchanged unless the sweep says otherwise. **Measure first:** the sweep. **Kill if:** 64 against 8 moves Desktop-High's `gpu main` by under 0.2 ms on the night tour. **Done when:** the sweep is in §3.9; if not killed, the rig fill keeps that saving with the character goldens inside their gates. | S | B9 | |
| P7 | **Half precision: a spike, then the colour assembly.** WGSL's `enable f16` with an f32 fallback from the same source (`crates/sapphire_render/src/shaders.rs`): `SHADER_F16` is requested on all three backends and used by no shader. First the phones' edge upscaler (F2's last step), then `shade_character` after the shadows and the rim (`anime_character.wgsl:1461-1547`) on Mobile-Low's `party-kinds`. Positions, depths, UV derivatives and shadow coordinates stay f32. **Gain:** unknown [U]: Apple describes the register saving without figures. Costs: dual paths, every golden on every backend, and shader model 6.2 on D3D12. Four tiers: phones first; desktops only if the spike says so. **Measure first:** the upscaler spike. **Kill if:** the spike saves under 10% of its pass, or the colour assembly's prototype under 3% of Mobile-Low's main pass. **Done when:** the spike's figures are in §3.9, and, if not killed, the colour assembly lands with its goldens re-baselined on three backends with the row's FLIP bound. | L | F2, B9 | |

### Track F: frame structure (exit tests 2, 3 and 5)

| ID | Package | Size | Depends on | As built |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | **The phones' output-size tail: the exact steps.** The edge upscaler's two passes run at the output's size whatever the scene's (spec §5.12, "As built (M5 L14)"): 575 and 117 µs at 2532×1170 in the fight, and a frame 0.475 ms dearer than bilinear on Mobile-High and 0.734 on Mobile-Low, about 16% and 23% of the fights' frames [M, logs]. Step 1, the exact flat path: where the four nearest texels are equal, return that value, byte-exact because the result is already clamped to their range (`crates/sapphire_render/shaders/post_upscale.wgsl:181-185`). Step 2: the interface and the debug draw draw in the last render pass (`crates/sapphire_render/src/ui.rs:772-784`, `crates/sapphire_render/src/post/upscale.rs:24-27`), not a pass of their own; the colour-vision view stays a pass of its own, since it filters the whole frame, the interface included, and so can't draw in the pass whose output it reads. Most output pixels lie in flat tone regions. **Gain:** part of F2's 0.2 to 0.35 ms on Mobile-High [E]; a flat path pays only for a 32-wide group wholly flat, not for a pixel [U]. Four tiers: the phones only; the desktops draw no upscaler. **Measure first:** the `upscaler` pair and B6's flat-group counter. **Kill if:** never; both steps are exact and small, and the counter decides F2. **Done when:** every golden is byte-identical, the `upscale-*` and phone scenes among them, and both phone tiers' fight frames with the two steps are in §3.9. | M | B6 | **As built (M6 F1):** both steps, exact (spec §5.12, "As built (M6 F1)"). Step 1: `fs_upscale_edge` returns the nearest texel where the four nearest are equal, skipping the votes and the kernel; the shader says the time is saved only by a wholly flat 32-wide group. Its twelve taps stay in their old order before the branch: reading the four first moved a pixel by a code value in two `upscale-*` scenes (Metal rounds the kernel's sum by the order its terms are defined). Step 2: on a frame that upscales along edges the interface and debug draw draw in the sharpen's render pass, after its triangle (`sapphire_render`'s `overlays` module), not in passes of their own; with a debug view on they keep their passes after it, and the colour-vision view stays the frame's last pass, a filter of all of it, reading what the sharpen wrote. The desktops and bilinear frames keep their passes, so the desktops' §3.3 `gpu ui` row is unchanged; on the phones it reads nothing and the sharpen's time holds the interface's, unless a dev sandbox's `--measure-unfolded-overlays` (B6's style) keeps their passes, so §3.3's interface row and the upscaler's own time read apart. Proved: every golden the base's to the byte (the full Metal set, 465 scenes); the renderer's GPU suite (455 tests); a GPU test that the folded frame is the separate passes' to the byte, a translucent quad and a masked one before the lines included, and the switch's frame too. B6's counter (counted, the `ci` cook, 2532 × 1170): wholly flat 8 × 4 groups are 4.1% of the district fight's on Mobile-High at p50 and 3.0% on Mobile-Low (4.3% and 3.3% over the run; the street look-dev 6.7% and 8.9%), under F2's 15% kill line. **Not done by this row yet:** the package landed ahead of its measured `upscaler` pair and its §3.9 figures, which the timed script owes (the pair and the district fight before and after on both phone tiers, folded and unfolded); the row stays open until §3.9 has them. |

### Track K: the cooker (exit tests 6 and 8)

What the cooker produces today, and what M5 measured each buying:

| Cooked product | Read by | Bought, measured |
|---|---|---|
| Quantized vertex streams, a stream of positions alone (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/mesh.rs`) | Every pass; depth and shadows read positions alone | Not paired |
| Level chains, deformation-aware, seams sealed, cache- and overdraw-ordered (`optimize.rs`) | `levels.rs`, `view_cull`, casters by cascade texel | 0.42 / 0.89 ms on Desktop-Low, at most 0.12 on phones, +0.08 to 0.23 ms of `render` [M, logs] |
| Character levels | `view_cull::choose_character_levels` | Nothing measurable [M, logs] |
| Merged cells and cell levels (`zone.rs`, `merge.rs`) | The streamer and draw runs | At most 0.2 ms [M, logs], for 13 to 17% more mesh pool (M5 D6) |
| SDF face maps, ramps, control maps, layer masks (`character_maps/`, `material.rs`) | The anime character shader's material group | The look |
| BC7, BC5, BC4 and BC6H on desktop, ASTC 4×4 on mobile, alpha-weighted levels (`texture.rs`, `texture/mips.rs`) | The material streamer | Memory; not paired |
| Probe grids and reflection keys (`probes.rs`, `reflections.rs`) | `ambient.rs` | The look; keys 315 and 79 MiB resident at 42 probes |
| Packs, zstd (`pack.rs`) | The asset server's mounts | A 5.6 MB patch holds the boot screen 5.4 to 5.6 s (M5 M7) [M] |

Two facts of the code shape the track. **No character material is alpha-tested:** content holds 77 opaque material documents, 5 blended and none masked, so hair and lashes are opaque, and the alpha-tested variants run only for the proximity fade, see-through regions, the dissolve and eyes (`crates/sapphire_assets/src/material/character.rs:1942-1948`). **Every shipped and benchmark character is a generated figure,** whose albedo is a cell a region with a gradient and optional grain and whose control map is exactly constant per region (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/figure/paint.rs:112-142`). Every row here keeps §3's cooker rules.

| ID | Package | Size | Depends on | As built |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| K1 | **A cook audit.** A `sapphire_pipeline` report written beside every cook, with no runtime change. Per material: which map channels are constant, constant per region or a gradient per region; whether the vertex's region ID (`VertexFlags::MATERIAL_ID_MASK`, `crates/sapphire_assets/src/mesh/encoding.rs:330-338`) agrees with the control map's ID over every triangle's texels; the census of alpha modes; and each map's bytes by texture class and cook. Per mesh: triangles and vertices at each level with its cooked error (the handoff's open question on the heroine's level 1, "Cooked levels save little"), the share of vertices the simplifier locks (morph targets, material seams, borders, `tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/optimize.rs:15-24`), and the reduction at an equal error with those locks relaxed. It acts on no frame row: it decides K3's depth, K4's reach and the coverage cook's trigger. **Measure first:** none. **Kill if:** never. **Done when:** the `budget` and shipped cooks write the report, byte for byte on a second cook, adding under 10% to the cook's time. | S | Plan | **As built (M6 K1, 2026-11-17):** `sapphire_pipeline::audit` writes `cook_audit.ron` (a versioned `CookAudit`) into each cook's loose folder, never packed; `optimize::audit_chain` counts LOD0's locked vertices by cause and builds the chain again unlocked. Packs are byte-identical to the base's on content/ and `ci` (30 files), and a second cook writes each report byte for byte from the cache. Timed in the cook's own process, cold, the audit is 0.47 s after a 20.2 s desktop cook of content/ (2.3%) and 0.52 s after a 16.1 s mobile one (3.3%); interleaved cold cooks, base then head twice under load 11 to 14, took 31 and 28 s against 28 and 29 s (desktop) and 24 and 27 s against 24 and 22 s (mobile). The `budget` cook, its bytes twice and its time with and without the audit are the orchestrator's timed run. Findings: every figure's control map (cast, ambient people, `ci`'s kinds) is constant per region, but for the warrior's G, with no triangle straddling two regions; none carries `_MATERIAL_ID`, so K4 writes the IDs from the map. The cast's levels lock 4.7% of vertices (morph targets) and only the heroine and Wren lock any; unlocked, their level 1 is 1.8% smaller, so the error budget, not the locks, bounds K3. The look-dev VRM locks 15% and halves its level 3 unlocked (9,953 to 4,934 triangles). Alpha-tested: only the nine foliage materials; blended: eyes, fog cards and the fountains' water. spec §5.27 "As built (M6 K1)". |
| K2 | **The pack cap.** `tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/pack.rs` writes parts of 64 to 128 MiB, or an overlay pack, read through the asset server's mounts (`crates/sapphire_assets/src/server/mounts.rs`). It acts on M5's M7 spike: a 5.6 MB patch to the district's 1.12 GB pack held the boot screen 5.4 to 5.6 s and the player 1.13 GB, the whole pack again ([m5-plan.md](m5-plan.md), M7's row) [M]. **Gain:** the hold under 0.6 s and the disk held under 128 MiB [E]; it also makes rule 5 affordable for every later format change. No goldens, no pipelines. **Measure first:** the M7 spike's script with the cap. **Kill if:** the hold stays over 2 s. **Done when:** the spike reads under 0.6 s (exit test 8), the pack format's version is bumped with its migration (an old pack read or refused with a clear error), the room check and staging scale with the parts a patch touches, and the patch tests pass. | M | Plan | **As built (M6 K2):** parts, not an overlay. A pack whose file would pass `PART_CAP` (128 MiB) is cut into parts, each an ordinary pack file with its own header, table and hashes: part 0 is `<name>.spack`, part `k` `<name>.<k>.spack` (`sapphire_assets::pack::{part_name, pack_of_part}`). Pack format 5 (`PACK_VERSION`, `PACKER` 5) puts the part and the count in header bytes 96 to 104, which format 4 kept zero, and the cut's stamp in 104 to 112; any other format is refused with what to do ("cook the content again", or for a newer one "read it with the build that cooked it"), since an app reads only its own cook or a patch made for it. The cut (`sapphire_pipeline::pack::cut`) keeps a texture with its levels, puts each unit in the bucket the first bits of its ID's blake3 name, takes the fewest buckets, a power of two, that each fit, and makes a part of each bucket, an empty one an empty part; a unit over the cap is a part alone. So an edit moves no asset between parts unless the bucket count changes. The asset server mounts a pack's parts as one, by the pack's name (`Parts` in `server/mounts.rs`), refuses parts that disagree on their place, count or cut's stamp or hold an ID twice, and remounts them as one; hot reload and live link map a part's file to its pack; the bench's `put_beside` links every part. The patcher needed no change: a manifest names each part as a pack, so the plan, the room check (`bytes_to_install`), staging and install already go by the parts a patch touches. `release --patch-from` refuses across pack formats ("ship this as an app build"), and live link counts a pack's parts as one pack. **[M, counts]** The `budget` district M7 rehearsed (format 4, 1,118,033,253 bytes, 2,666 assets), read and written again at the cap with its stored blocks kept (no new cook): 16 parts of 55.3 to 90.8 MB (52.8 to 86.6 MiB). M7's small patch (the same texture and its four levels, by M7's seed) changes one part, `zone_district.2` (84,921,096 bytes), and `release --patch-from` fetches 3,354,953 bytes, its tables 174,320 where M7's were 2,441,552 for the whole district. Through `sapphire_patch`'s API phase by phase (M7's harness, against `patch serve`), the download staged 3.5 MB in 94 files, the install wrote and verified that one part (84,921,096 bytes, against M7's 1,118,044,133) and the patch directory peaked at 88.5 MB against M7's 1.13 GB; the room asked is that part and the 64 MiB reserve. Tests: an old pack refused with its message, a part's header and name agreeing, the cut never parting a texture from its levels with every part under the cap, one texture's edit changing one part, a unit over the cap alone, a cook's pack in parts read back, mounted, loaded and packed again to the same bytes, parts mounted, loaded and remounted as one and a disagreeing set refused, and `a_patch_to_one_part_installs_that_part_alone` (one of four parts fetched, its bytes to install and the one file installed, under a quarter of the whole pack's install). **Owed:** the boot screen's hold for that patch (exit test 8, under 0.6 s; kill over 2 s) is the orchestrator's timed run. **Limits:** parts run 53 to 87 MiB on the district, a little under the row's 64. Every part's header holds the count and the stamp, so an edit that changes them rewrites every part and its patch installs the whole pack: one that changes the bucket count or adds or removes a unit over the cap; emptying or filling a bucket doesn't, since an empty bucket is an empty part. Live link can't push a pack whose part count changed a part at a time. Spec §5.10, "As built (M6 K2)". |
| K3 | **Per-level triangle constants.** The level chain already stores each level's error (`optimize.rs:30-35`); the cooked mesh gains each level's front-facing triangle count and projected area at unit distance, which P5 divides into pixels per triangle. A mesh format version (rule 2); no pipeline. If K1 shows a high locked share, deeper character levels need the locks relaxed before this pays. **Measure first:** P5's. **Kill if:** P5 is killed. **Done when:** P5 is done, and an old mesh is read or refused with a clear error. | S | K1 | |
| K4 | **Fold control maps that are constant per region into the material.** Where K1 shows a figure's control map constant per region and its vertex IDs agreeing, the material cook writes each region's highlight mask, shadow bias and highlight size into its `CharacterRegion` (`crates/sapphire_assets/src/material/character.rs:700`; it carries `specular_size` today, neither bias nor mask) and leaves the control slot unbound; the shader takes its existing branch for no control map, which reads the region from the vertex (`anime_character.wgsl:1206-1216`), once that branch reads the two new fields. A GPU block's layout changes and no permutation is added: `material_has_texture` is a uniform branch. It acts on the character pixel: the control map's two filtered reads and its unfiltered ID read, 3 of about 17, and a manual trilinear blend, on every generated figure (the hero, the party and the district fight's twelve). **Gain:** 0.1 to 0.3 ms of Desktop-High's `gpu main` in the district fight [E, low]; about 0.05 on phones. Not additive with P4: after P4 it saves two reads, not three. **It holds only for generated figures,** whose control maps are constant per region by construction, so the benchmark flatters it; authored characters with painted control detail won't qualify, and K1 says which do. On the figures it folds it also ends the generator's control-map bleed at coarse levels ([m6-handoff.md](m6-handoff.md), "The look"). Four tiers alike. **Measure first:** B6's unbound-control-slot switch on `df-terms` base. **Kill if:** Desktop-High's `gpu main` falls by under 0.1 ms. **Done when:** the district fight's main pass falls by the probe's figure; character goldens are re-baselined at region seams, where filtered control values blended across borders and now don't, with the row's FLIP bound; one format version (rule 2). | M | K1, B9, P4 | |

It lands before B9, so every M6 figure is on it. **Measure first:** none. **Kill if:** never. **Done when:** the workspace's pin and lock move, every golden is byte-identical on three backends (one that moves is the PR's to explain and re-baseline), the advisories pass, and the GPU and device-loss tests pass. | S | Plan | **As built (M6 X1):** the pin and the lock moved the whole wgpu family, nine crates, from 30.0.1 to 30.0.2; `wgpu` alone changes only its WebGPU backend, so both fixes come with `wgpu-core`. Both are read in the crates' sources [S] (they ship no changelog, so wgpu's issue numbers stay [U]): a buffer destroyed while mapped keeps its GPU buffer until the wgpu buffer drops, and an acquire that returns no texture frees its texture ID. The belts keep their chunks mapped between frames, so a loss destroys mapped buffers; today no view of ours spans that destroy (each opens and drops on the polling thread, and a destroyed buffer gives no new view), so the first fix guards a future poll from another thread. ### The production tracks

The ten tracks below carry the production areas (§1, "What the production tracks add"): T, C, W, A and U, then G, H, Y, V and N, which build on W's rows and amend some of them in place. Their rows keep the form and the usual rules above, and add these:

- **Measure first** appears where a feature's cost could miss its row, priced on W1's nature zone, B4's district, B5's fight, an offline render or a count; **Kill if** where a cost could be too high or a feature buy too little. A feature with neither is proved by its **Done when** alone. The rows are shorter than the performance rows: each package's brief draws its detail from the research behind them.
- No production package lands inside a timed window unless its unit is in B9's manifest, and the GPU look features (W3b onward, and every G, H, Y, V and N package that draws) are scheduled after §5's gate.
- A row that waits on the maintainer (a crate, an SDK, a host, a licence, a person) says **Waits on §7**.
- Every golden from before a production package stays byte-identical unless its row names a re-baseline: W7's `wet-*` scenes, A4's retargeted clips, A7's `lookdev-graph-run` and C8's Mobile-High cloth and spring scenes. About 14 new Metal scenes come from W, about 10 from A, and C6's and C9's, and about 50 to 60 from G, H, Y, V and N, counting a scene a tier [E]; T and U add none, since the editor proves its panes against captures and audio draws nothing. W1's nature zone is re-pinned in each pull request that fills one of its areas, and the orchestrator re-baselines that area's units in the next quiet window.
- W3, W6, W10, W13, W15, W16 and W19 declare new pipeline keys in their rows, each built only when a zone needs it: about 8 to 11 a desktop tier and 5 to 8 a phone tier in a zone with every feature [E]. G, H, Y, V and N's are in §5's "Pipelines". A21, W4 (under water by the fog's uniforms), W7, W8 and W9, and the T, C, A and U tracks, add none.
- A row that moves a document's version, the zone's cooked version, an asset kind number, the live-link protocol or the save's version says so; they move in landing order (§3, "Seams between tracks").

### Track T: the editor and authoring tools (exit test 9)

`tools/sapphire_editor` (about 11,700 lines, `app.rs` alone 2,473) and `tools/sapphire_document` (about 5,300) hold 344 tests between them: a reflected inspector, a translate gizmo with one undo step a drag, a sequencer that plays, a screen pane 0 pixels from its 30 goldens, and live link (protocol 6), through which a running build takes a saved document in 0.12 to 0.30 s (spec §5.29). Seven document kinds open (`tools/sapphire_document/src/kinds.rs:139-199`) of content's thirty-odd suffixes. **The design:** commands first, so every later pane is scriptable and provable headless; then steps across documents, a journal, more kinds and cooking from the editor; then the world editor (IDs, layers, every zone object drawn and picked, gizmo modes); then the visual panes, each proved by "the pane equals the capture". The look pane's centre is the day's keys and light rigs as curves, ramps as gradient stops and the four tiers side by side, not a material node graph: the studio behind one of the reference games describes curves that set character brightness through the day [U: an Epic developer interview, 2024, secondhand]. **Seams:** T gives the other tracks the pane slot (T1), the curve widget (T14), the node canvas (T17a), zone markers and handles (T9a, T9b) and binary-document undo (T13a); it reads C2's index for thumbnails, W14's edits for brushes, A5's document and A9's snapshot for the graph pane, and hosts U12's audio pane. No T package touches the shipped frame, the simulation or a golden; T4 and T8 change document types under the cooker's rules. The editor's rows (a UI pass at rest under 2 ms at p95, a pick under 1 ms, a gizmo edit drawn within 33 ms at p95, save to pixel through a cook within 2 s at the median, a stage stood in 300 ms, one journal write a step) are [E] until T12 measures them, as a new spec §3 row, "Editor".

| ID | Package | Size | Depends on | As built |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T0 | **The first window.** The maintainer opens the editor on macOS and walks the list of what only a window shows (`docs/development-iteration.md:146-165`: the window, focus, a gizmo drag, the sequencer, the screen pane, Play, closing with edits), recorded by T1's `--record` if it has landed; an agent fixes each finding with a kittest that reproduces it. **Done when:** the list is walked once on macOS and each fix has its test; Windows and Linux stay owed. **Waits on §7.** | S (agent side) | Plan | |
| T1 | **Commands.** A `CommandRegistry`: every button, menu item, drag end and shortcut runs `Editor::run` with a named command and RON arguments (open, save, undo, set a property, a gesture's begin and end, select, play, push, a cvar, a pane opened or closed); a palette (Cmd+P); `--run <file.commands.ron>` headless with `--capture`, and `--record`. Blender makes operators the unit of action and undo for the same reason [U: Blender's developer documentation]. `app.rs` splits by pane on the way. **Done when:** every kittest in `tests/ui.rs`, `panes.rs` and `docking.rs` has a command-script twin reaching the same document bytes, and a recorded session replays to the same bytes and capture. | M | Plan | **As built (M6 T1):** `sapphire_editor::commands`: a `Command` for each thing the interface does, a `CommandRegistry` of their names, help, templates and keys, and `Editor::run`, which every button, menu item, drag and shortcut calls and which says why it refuses. A script is `(version: 1, commands: [...])` in a `.commands.ron`, refusing unknown fields and versions; `--run` plays one with no window (with `--capture` after it), `--record` keeps a window's session, and Cmd+P is the palette. `app.rs` split into the top bar, the timeline's panes, the inspector, the device pane and the dispatch. `tests/scripts.rs` twins all 75 kittests to the same bytes (the device kittests' with no device), and a kittest-driven session recorded and replayed by the binary gives the same bytes and the same PNG. Spec §5.28. |
| T2 | **Selection across documents, multi-edit and set steps.** A selection of objects by document and path; the inspector edits the fields a selection shares; a set-level `SetStep` holds each touched document's step, so per-document stacks stay and match per-file save (the shape of Godot 4's editor undo [U: its documentation]). A placement moves with its named colliders, which the cook refuses apart (M4 X1), as one undo. **Done when:** a fuzz of mixed steps undoes every file to its bytes, a placement-and-collider move cooks clean, and a three-placement edit is one undo. | M | T1 | **As built (M6 T2):** each step of a document's history has a `StepId`, and a `DocumentSet` keeps `SetStep`s over the documents' own stacks: a label and each touched document's step by its ID, so each document's undo, dirty state, per-file save and journal entry (T3) stay its own. `DocumentSet::together` makes one from a closure's edits (a refusal inside takes back only what it began) and a set gesture (`begin_gesture`, `edit`, `end_gesture`) from a drag's frames, the editor's `Begin` and `End`; only an edit that changed two documents or more makes one. Undo or redo of a document whose next step is a set step's part moves every part when each is its document's next, and otherwise is refused naming the document whose own later step is in the way, nothing moved (`HistoryError::Later`); a part no longer where undo or redo would find it (its document closed, the cap or a rebase let it go, or it was taken on its own) refuses a bound step both ways (`Gone`), the step a placement makes with its colliders, and lets any other go in this document alone (`Took::Apart`), which the editor says, naming what it left. A save of a document also saves the documents a set step joins it to, so a zone saved never leaves its colliders unsaved. Colliders are a kind (`.colliders.ron`, sixteen in all; `ColliderDocument` reflects, its serde and cooks unchanged), and `edit_with_colliders` runs a zone edit and moves the colliders each moved placement or instance names by the same distance, worked in f64 so a collider at its placement's number lands to the bit; turning or scaling a placement moves none (T9b), and an edit that adds or takes out placements or instances moves none, since their places shift. The editor's selection is objects by document and path (`ObjectId`), in any open documents: a Cmd or Shift click in the zone pane adds a row or takes it out, the gizmo is on the last, and with more than one the Properties pane lists the fields all of them have (a value where they agree), one edit setting it under each as one step (`SelectObjects`, `SetShared`). Every zone edit, typed or dragged, takes the named colliders. The document scanner reads RON's raw names (`r#None`), which the writer gives a variant called `None` (a collider's `Rotation::None`). Proof: a fuzz of mixed steps over five documents of four kinds (own steps, set steps, set gestures, refused edits, random undo and redo, saves between) undoes every file to its bytes; every shipped colliders document (5) fuzzes to its bytes; a placement moved by the editor's call cooks with its collider, and its undo cooks; the three-placement kittest and its script twin are one undo of both files; a journal brings back each document a set step touched. No golden or replay can move: nothing the runtime reads changed. Left: the set step isn't journaled (each document comes back with its own steps); a window's Cmd-click is proved only through kittest input. |
| T3 | **The journal.** After each step a dirty document's text goes to `target/editor_journal/` through a temporary and a rename, with the file's disk hash; at start it is offered back, and one whose file moved on is offered as a conflict through the document model's rebase, never written over a source. **Done when:** a test kills an editor child mid-gesture and a new editor offers every unsaved text unchanged, and a file changed on disk meanwhile comes back as a conflict. | S | T1 | **As built (M6 T3):** `sapphire_document::journal`: each editor a session folder under `target/editor_journal/`, locked while it runs; after each command an entry for each document whose unsaved text changed (its text, the blake3 of the bytes its edits began on, and history's steps past the save, a gesture under way the last), written through a temporary and a rename. Sessions that ended without closing are offered in the top bar; `Recover` takes one back by `DocumentSet::recover` (its steps the document's undo, checked against the hash), dirty, or in conflict with a file that moved on, which `MineOnTop` settles by the rebase; `DropRecovered` lets it go; no source is written but by a save. `--journal <folder>` names another place, and `--run` keeps one only when told. `tests/journal.rs` kills an editor child mid-drag with three documents unsaved and a new editor offers all three unchanged, the one saved meanwhile as a conflict; the kittest has its script twin. A value still being typed isn't kept until it lands. Spec §5.28. |

| T5 | **Cooking from the editor.** After a save (debounced 300 ms, one cook at a time, the newest request winning) the editor spawns the `tools`-profile pipeline binary, since a tool can't be linked, and remounts the stage's packs; counters `editor.cook_ms` and `editor.cooks_failed`. **Measure first:** the pipeline's process start against a cook, which takes 584 to 661 ms from its start (spec §5.29); over 30% and a long-lived server waits on its trigger (§3). **Done when:** a prefab never cooked draws once its placement is saved, with no manual step; a broken material shows its refusal by field; desktop packs stay byte-identical. | M | T1 | **As built (M6 T5):** measured first (Apple M5, load about 9): the pipeline's process start is 5.5 ms median (4.4 to 9.3) against a no-op editor cook of 2.6 to 3.2 s, 0.2%, so no long-lived server; most of a no-op cook is keying the 105 keypose clips (2.0 s), and the 584 to 661 ms the row cites is out of date. `sapphire_editor::cook`: a `Cooker` takes each save of a document under the content tree the cook reads, waits for 300 ms with no other (a newer one waits again), runs one cook at a time through a `Runner` (the `tools`-profile `target/tools/sapphire_pipeline`, spawned with `cargo xtask content`'s own arguments for that cook, no generators and no `--locked`, under the same `<packs>.lock`) and, asked again while one runs, runs one more after it, so the newest save wins; a lock some other cook holds is waited out the same way. `CookStats` gives `editor.cook_ms` and `editor.cooks_failed` (R9), logged at each end. The pipeline's `cook --refusal <file>` writes why a cook failed as a document (`sapphire::pipeline::CookRefusal` v1: the source, the field as a property path and the message, through a temporary and a rename) and takes it away after a clean one; a material's texture slot names its field (`MaterialDocument::texture_fields`, so `base_color_map` or `surface[0].mask_map`). A `--run` script settles its cooks before it ends; `cargo xtask editor` passes `--pipeline`. Proof: unit tests for the debounce, one at a time, newest-wins with the lock held, the counters and a cooker that isn't there; `tests/cook.rs` (ignored, needing this tree's pipeline) refuses a material bound to a texture no source has, naming `base_color_map` on the top bar, in Properties and on the row, and cooks clean once it's taken back, and an editor cook of `content/` writes every file `cargo xtask content --cook editor` wrote, byte for byte; `tests/viewport_stage.rs` saves a placement naming a prefab no cook had seen and it draws with no other step, its pipelines built before first use and the pane within temporal AA's history of a capture on the new cook (7,394 of 226,200 pixels by at most 29: the pane drew the wait's frames). ### Track C: the artist content pipeline (exit test 10)

The engine's half of the pipeline is built: glTF, FBX and VRM import with the anime data (`COLOR_0` as AO, outline width and Z offset; `_MATERIAL_ID`; `_OUTLINE_NORMAL`; material extras, `tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/import/gltf.rs:15-42`), a blake3-keyed content cache, local only (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/cache.rs:75-148`), character maps from documents, three headless Blender scripts (MIT), and budgets held only by tests (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/figure/tests.rs:283-308`). Every shipped and benchmark character is generated; the one authored character, pixiv's sample VRM, is over spec §3.4 on slots (13) and mobile joints (155). A `content/` cook takes 16 s cold and about 156 ms when nothing changed; the `budget` district 249 s on desktop and 199 s on mobile. **The design:** no new crate and no tool in the cook path (§3, "The pipeline's shape"). `sapphire_pipeline` gains `check`, `index`, `scaffold`, `variant`, `skeleton` and `remote`; a Blender add-on exports glTF with this engine's anime data and shells out to the pipeline for every rule; `cargo xtask content --watch` over `sapphire_watch` closes the loop to hot reload and live link with no daemon (a warm cook with one changed source is 0.06 s on 200 meshes plus the asset's own, spec §5.27). Arc System Works' artists saw lines and shading in the modelling tool as they edited [U: their GDC 2015 talk]: the engine has the data and lacks that loop. For a team, a read-through cache client over `ureq` (already the patcher's) against bazel-remote or any blob store, written only by `main`'s CI and named machines, once the encoder family is in every recipe whose bytes differ by architecture, with a nightly audit because the key leaves out the pipeline binary. **Seams:** K1 owns the cook audit and C2 the index that folds it in; C9's variant documents are what W19 draws; C8 follows A's rule for clip keys on dropped joints; T10 reads C2's thumbnails. Every row keeps §3's cooker rules and adds no shipping pipeline. C12 adds cook-time rows on the M5 Mac for both cooks: nothing changed (under 0.3 s at ten times today's sources with C11's stat cache [E]), one hero recooked (under 15 s on desktop [E]), `content/` cold within 10% of today, and `budget` cold.

| ID | Package | Size | Depends on | As built |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | **`sapphire_pipeline check`: roles, rules and waivers.** A sidecar's optional `budget: (role, waive)`, written only when set, so every committed sidecar keys byte for byte; each role's limits are §3.4's rows in one table (the hero 80k / 30k triangles, 200 / 140 joints, 6 / 4 slots after merges, 2K / 1K a map; enemy, boss and ambient proposed), read from a cook of the one mesh to memory and `texture::cooked_size` without encoding. Lints beside them: unbound slots, a map over 1K with no mobile cap, VRoid-style names that silently fell back to default eye layers or region light (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/import/vrm.rs:278`, `:303`), duplicate spring-chain names, non-ASCII stems, duplicate texture bytes; text and `--json`. The content tests call it, so CI and an artist apply one rule set. **Done when:** `check content` passes with the look-dev VRM's two written waivers, the cast passes at its roles, each rule's fixture fails by name, and every sidecar keys as before. | M | Plan | **As built (M6 C1, 2026-11-17):** `sapphire_pipeline check <dir> [--json]` (`sapphire_pipeline::check`) reads a tree as a locked cook resolves it and writes nothing. A sidecar's `budget: (role: Hero, waive: [(rule: Slots, why: "…")])` sits beside `id` and `importer`, outside every cache key, written only when set. Each role mesh cooks to memory on both cooks, merges included (`cook::cook_mesh_of`, shared with `cook_mesh_in`), for LOD0's triangles, the skin's joints and the slots after merges; its bound materials' maps are sized by `texture::cooked_size` under their caps, a generated map generated but never encoded. `check::ROWS` is one table: the hero is §3.4's row; proposed for the maintainer, every role takes the per-character rows (200 / 140 joints, 6 / 4 slots, 2K / 1K maps), enemies 8,000 triangles and bosses 20,000 on both cooks (the phone draws the desktop's LOD1, which the mobile cook keeps by skipping LOD0: the Ember Lancer's is 6,610 of 8,000, so half would fail it), the ambient cast 4,000 on both, 64 joints, 2 / 1 slots and 1K / 512 maps. Each rule's fixture fails by name (`check::tests`). A waiver marks its findings, which still print with the reason; every cook lists every waiver (`CookReport::waivers`) and none judges. | C3 | **The Blender add-on.** `tools/blender/sapphire/` (MIT, §3): an export operator over Blender's glTF exporter with the engine's settings; panels to paint `_MATERIAL_ID` on face corners and the data colour (AO, outline width, Z offset) with a preview of the hull's width, to transfer smoothed normals into `_OUTLINE_NORMAL` and to edit the material extras; the mask bake folded in; Check running C1 through the pipeline binary. Each run writes the same bytes, from the checkout or the installed zip, after removing four sources of run-to-run change (face order after bmesh deletes, threaded vertex normals, nearest-surface ties, quad diagonals). Blender isn't on CI: the add-on itself is proved by running the script. |
| C4 | **Cook on save.** `cargo xtask content --watch` over `sapphire_watch`: the incremental cook on each debounced change, errors printed without stopping; the running sandbox's hot reload remounts its packs and `cargo xtask link watch` carries them to a simulator; the add-on's Send exports into the content tree. **Done when:** a scripted edit recooks exactly that asset; a re-export of the test character shows in a `--capture` sandbox started before it (by counts); the orchestrator times edit to screen on desktop and a simulator (under 3 s warm [E]). | S | Plan; C3 for Send | **As built (M6 C4):** `cargo xtask content --watch [--cook <cook>] [--report]` (`xtask/src/content/watch.rs`): after its cook, `sapphire_watch`'s `FileWatcher` on every folder of `content/` but hidden ones, new folders watched before each cook, a folder gone counted as a change, and any error of the watcher's (dropped events among them) a cook too; symbolic links never followed, as a cook refuses them; each change runs the same pipeline `cook` as a process under the cook's lock, held for that cook alone, and a failed cook is printed while the watch goes on. No daemon, no second cooker, nothing new in the apps: hot reload already remounts a rewritten pack, and `link watch` already pushes one. The add-on's **Send** exports into the scene's content folder and refuses a file outside it; every export now writes a hidden file renamed into place (the same bytes), which no cook reads half-written. `tests/cook_on_save.rs`: a re-exported test character in a scratch tree of committed sources recooks its mesh and skeleton alone, and only its pack is written again, with only the mesh compressed anew. By counts, on `content/` with the test character in it (a debug `dev` sandbox `--offscreen --look-dev enemy --model` on it, started before the edit; the add-on's Send headless in Blender 5.2): the watch cooked 2 of 1,342 assets, re-encoded 1 of `shared.spack`'s 817 and left the other 12 packs up to date; the sandbox remounted `shared` (1 changed, 1 reloading) and swapped the mesh in as generation 1. On the iOS Simulator (the dev sandbox through `xtask run --platform ios-sim`, `content --watch --cook mobile` and `link watch`), the same save sent 11.3 KB of the 15.7 MB mobile `shared` pack and the mesh came back as generation 1. Under a load of 6 to 10, a warm one-asset cook took 2.8 to 4.0 s, 2.15 s of 2.8 of it keying the 105 key-pose clips, none changed (each document is parsed whole to learn whether it names morphs); 0.24 s packing. Each map starts as what the shader read without it (the ramp a row per region of its band tint, the ILM the unbound values, a layer mask 1), so the character draws nearly as before but for the face (and a face's or hair's imported layer mask, a body's alone, is unbound): the ramp and ILM store their values in 8 bits, and band mode's softened deep step is a hard edge in the ramp, with no test comparing the looks; a slot of several regions gets its ILM written but not bound, since a constant ID would override its vertices' IDs, and the command names it. Every texture and map sidecar it makes is capped at its role's row (`max_size`, at most 1K on mobile), so `map-size` and `uncapped-map` hold as made. Never overwrites: a second run writes nothing, a bound slot is left alone, and a sidecar of another importer where one would go, or another model under the model's name, is an error naming the file, as are a `--part` naming no slot and a copied model naming files beside it; the whole run is planned before its first write, so a refusal writes nothing. One ID generator, `meta::IdSource` (the OS's randomness, or seeded for tests), which the cook's sidecars and extraction draw from too. `ImportPreset` (`<name>.preset.ron`, v1): a sidecar's importer and settings and an optional `budget`, copied into a sidecar when it is made and never read again; built in `vrm-character`, `gltf-character`, `fbx-character` (the cook's own mesh settings, cast as heroes), and a cook's new VRM sidecar starts from `vrm-character` (a glTF or FBX gets the defaults: a cook can't tell a character from a prop). | C6 | **Face maps an artist can judge.** The face SDF document's `edges: AntiAliased` reads masks' grey edges through the edge-distance transform the code lists as later (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/character_maps.rs:48-50`), `Binary` staying the default; `bake-map --sheet` draws the map thresholded at chosen light angles beside the masks, the two things commercial face-shadow tools advertise over this method [U: a face-shadow tool's guide]. **Measure first:** at 512² masks, the edge's error against a 2048² binary reference. **Kill if:** the anti-aliased edge isn't measurably closer. **Done when:** every existing face map is byte-identical and one new face golden shows a 512² anti-aliased face at three light angles. | S | Plan | **As built (M6 C6):** `character_maps::face_sdf`. `edges: AntiAliased` reads a mask's gray edge texels through Gustavson and Strand's anti-aliased distance transform (the exact transform's nearest touched texel, then its 3 × 3 neighbors by their coverage's edge offset); the same texels light and turn between the same masks, only where the values cross between texels moves. `Binary` stays the default and isn't written, so the document stays version 1 and its importer at its version: the four committed face maps bake byte-identical to the base (`bake-map`, compared). **Measured** (a test that holds it): at 512² masks against a 2048² binary reference, read bilinearly as the shader reads it, the edge's mean error is 0.120 texels binary and 0.020 anti-aliased on a wavy band, 0.130 and 0.020 on shrinking discs. `bake-map --sheet <degrees,…>` draws a face map lit from the head's right and left at each angle beside the document's mask there, red where the map misses it (the allowed violations); a test holds its size and layout. The golden `faces-anti-aliased` draws the sample head with a twin face map from the same fields at 512² (`face_edges_*`, `masks_edges/`), lit at 55°, 75° and 95°, each between two masks. The add-on showing the sheet is C3's. It can't sharpen a mask painted with hard edges, and a texel the edge runs through may turn at its mask's own angle once the cook rounds it. |
| C7 | **Ramps painted as images.** A `.ramp.ron` row may be `Image((path, row))`, a PNG strip sampled at texel centres in the document's blend space; version 2 reads version 1 unchanged. **Done when:** a strip and its equivalent stops cook within one code value, and existing ramps are byte-identical. | S | Plan | **As built (M6 C7, 2026-11-17):** `RampMap` is at version 2: each ramp is `Stops((…))` or `Image((path, row))` (`character_maps::ramp::{Ramp, StopRamp, ImageRamp}`), and a version 1 document, all stops, reads through `ramp::v1` and bakes the same rows. A strip is a PNG (or TGA or EXR), sRGB colour with linear alpha, named relative to the document; its texels are stops at their centres, blended in the document's interpolation, so a strip as wide as the bake (256 by default) is copied texel for texel. A strip wider than the bake and a row the image lacks are refused, naming the ramp (`RampError::ImageWidth`, `ImageRow`); a `row` left out is a document error naming it. The strip is an input like a mask: no sidecar, claimed out of the cook, a file dependency of the ramp's key, so editing it recooks that ramp alone. The ramp stage stays at version 2 and doesn't carry the image importer's version, so no committed ramp's key moved; the image importer's version note says to bump the ramp stage with it. The swatch sample is still written at version 1 (its bytes key its cook). Proved: a strip painted from four typed ramps (a committed 32 × 4 fixture, its texels checked against the test's stops) cooks within one code value of them at its width and copies texel for texel; at 32, 100 and 256 texels, in all four blend spaces, it cooks within one code value of its texels as stops (bands exactly); all 22 committed ramps keep their keys and cooked bytes on both cooks (compared against the base). Nothing shipped uses an image row, so no golden moves. Not done: the editor's ramp pane (T15a), the Blender add-on painting strips (C3). spec §5.27 "Ramps". |
