### Track W: the world's look (exit test 11; GPU features after §5's gate)

The world's light is built: environment shading with wet surfaces whose puddles reflect probes (M5 L4), weather as two shares (`crates/sapphire_look/src/weather.rs:19-24`), sky, fog, shafts, the probe grid and box-corrected reflection probes, and a foliage family with wind for hand-placed plants. Rain itself, ripples, streaks, shelter and wet characters are "later" (`crates/sapphire_render/src/environment.rs:52-53`); water, terrain, screen-space reflections, impostors, photo mode, costume variants and the external upscalers don't exist, and static colliders are convex shapes or meshes only (`crates/sapphire_physics/src/world.rs:49-56`). **The design:** each piece takes the renderer's existing shape: a material family, cooked data in the zone's presentation half, a pipeline declared only when a zone needs it, a counter, and one small simulation half where gameplay must read the world. **Water** is family six with a painted look (depth bands from a ramp row, a foam line from a distance field, two scrolling normal maps, footprint-sized sparkles, the probe or sky in reflection) over a **cooked top-down bed depth per body**, since Mobile-Low never has a readable depth (Unity's mobile Boat Attack sample captures shore depth from above for the same reason [U]); swimming reads `WaterVolumes::at` in the zone's simulation half, a flat rest surface, with waves a presentation offset. **Rain and snow** are a stateless pass after the resolve, each drop a hash of its index, a camera-centred cell and a time period through N8a's shared include, bent by Y1's wind: one draw, no state, so neither the compute particles §3 rejects nor a share of combat's CPU caps. A **cooked shelter map** per cell stops drops under roofs and dries puddles there, the spec's own complaint about L4. **Reflections:** masked SSR at half resolution on Desktop-High and quarter on Desktop-Low, against the prepass depth into the last resolved frame, falling back to the probe and sky; this look's puddles are mirror-flat, so a plain march is enough. **Terrain** follows §3's decision; **grass** is placed on the CPU in chunks around the camera by hash dithering over a density map, Guerrilla's method done on the jobs pool [U: their GDC 2017 talk], in place of spec §5.12's GPU grass, which §3's rejection of the GPU-driven path overrules, and drawn through G2a's foliage field; **far trees** are hemi-octahedral impostors baked at cook by the probes' ray caster, G2b's zone-resident set. **MetalFX** and **DLSS** sit behind `TemporalInputs` with the responsive mask as their reactive mask, each on §7's yes. **Photo mode** is a game screen over systems that exist; **costume variants** draw C9's documents. Proposed rows for spec §3.3 (W0), all [E] from measured neighbours, Desktop-High / Desktop-Low / Mobile-High / Mobile-Low in ms: water 0.5 / 0.45 / 0.5 / 0.4 at about 40% of the frame, its refraction copy 0.1 / 0.08 on the desktops; terrain 0.8 / 0.7 / 0.8 / 0.6; grass with its trail 1.0 / 0.8 / 0.8 / 0.4; impostors 0.2 / 0.2 / 0.2 / 0.15; precipitation 0.25 / 0.25 / 0.3 / 0.25; rain on surfaces +0.1 on the first three; SSR 0.6 / 0.4 on the desktops. A nature zone spends them instead of the district's building load (§3, "Production costs"). Weather, precipitation, reflections, terrain's drawing, grass and impostors are presentation; water volumes and terrain collision move the digest only of zones that use them. G, H, Y, V and N add their own rows to W0's list. **Seams:** W14's edits are what T13b's brushes call; A19 swims in W2's volumes and W5 draws its ring events; W8b's lightning sends U's thunder cue; W6 lands after F6 and draws where F6 leaves effects; W9 and W10's character read join the anime character shader's line after K4 (§5). G, H, Y, V and N build on these rows, and their changes to W2 to W10 and W12 to W16 are written into the rows below, not repeated in theirs.

### Track A: animation (exit test 12)

The line the genre needs is already drawn: the simulation owns position, timing, hit volumes and movement, presentation owns the pose, and root motion reaches the simulation only as tables cooked from the same deterministic clip samples, so a pose and its hit volume agree on a tick (`crates/sapphire_body_pose/src/graph.rs:38-41`). What's missing is what animators reach for: clips carry no foot contacts, events or mirroring; an FBX clip "isn't the same bytes", so no move may bake from one (`tools/sapphire_pipeline/src/clip.rs:22-24`); the graph exists but has no blend spaces over parameters, nested machines, sync or events; the fight never feeds `ReactionInput`; `ControllerDrive::flight` exists and nothing calls it (`crates/sapphire_gameplay/src/controller/mod.rs:227`); faces bind morphs but not VRM's texture and colour binds. **The design keeps the line:** intake first (clip v2, FBX curves in deterministic Rust, BVH, contact-preserving retargeting), then **graph v2**, a compiled node document whose v1 conversion evaluates bit for bit what v1 did, then presentation-only fits (stride and orientation warping, directional reactions from `HitEvent`'s point and normal), then **ragdolls on a small XPBD rigid-body solver in `sapphire_anim`,** presentation only, beside the cloth solver that already runs on the owner clock (§3), then **traversal in tiers** as simulation (§3), and **faces** that complete VRM 1.0's expression model. A new "Physical animation" row of 0.3 / 0.2 / 0.2 / 0.4 ms (Mobile-Low at 30 fps) with caps of 4, 2, 2 and 1 active ragdolls is the only new cost row [E]; graph v2 lives inside the animation row's 30-fold headroom (spec §3), capped by its compiler at 8 clips sampled a character (as built, 12 an evaluation of the layers: A5's "As built (M6 A5a)"). **Only A14 moves checksums,** by its row (§3, "Traversal, and how far"); A18's anchors and A19's water move digests only of zones that have them. **Seams:** A6 owns surface tags for footsteps, which U4's cue switches on; A9's snapshot is what T17b's pane reads; A19 swims in W2's volumes; A21 joins the anime character shader's line after K4; A24's tables are read by U14's cook stage; A10 lands after J1, since both edit the stage's secondary-motion scheduling.

| ID | Package | Size | Depends on | As built |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A0 | **The decisions, written.** Spec §5.14 and §5.11 say traversal's tiers, enemy ragdolls on our own solver (its "swap in Jolt" replaced) and motion matching out (§3); the new row proposed in §3.3. No code. **Done when:** the spec says it. | S | Plan | **As built (M6 A0):** the spec says it. §5.14's "swap in Jolt (via `joltc`)" is replaced by ragdolls on the engine's own XPBD solver, whose design, tier caps, graph v2, motion matching left out and the hit direction's rule are §5.13's; traversal's tiers are §5.22's, beside the controller whose `GroundMode` they join. The "Physical animation" row, a proposal of 0.3 / 0.2 / 0.2 / 0.4 ms, is a CPU row, so it is in §3.2, not §3.3; and this row's "§5.11" is `sapphire_rhi`, so §5.13 and §5.22 carry what it meant. Documents only. |
| A1 | **Clip format v2.** Per-foot marker tracks (down, up) detected at cook by height and speed with hysteresis; event tracks for notifies; mirroring by the humanoid map; a per-clip report (slide on planted ticks, loop seam, bound radius) that C1's check and the clip inspector read; the clip's version bumped with v1 read. **Done when:** every shipped clip cooks v2 with contacts; the hash test covers a clip with markers; side dodges cook mirrored; no golden moves. | M | Plan | **As built (M6 A1):** a cooked clip is version 2 (stage `sclip` 7): each foot's contact track (`Clip::contacts`, on a rig whose map names the feet: planted at the start, `Down`/`Up` markers, the slide while planted), events (`Clip::events`, from a sidecar's or key-pose document's `events:`) and a report (`Clip::report`: loop seam and bound radius, from the samples before reduction); spec §5.13's "As built (M6 A1)" has the rules. Contacts land a sole under 3 cm above its rest and 0.3 m/s, lift past 1.5 times either, close lifts and drop inside contacts under 3 ticks; a clip that runs in place lands by vertical speed only, since `Running_B`'s planted foot speeds from 5.3 to 8.0 m/s through its contact. A version 1 clip is read by its frozen layout (`clip::v1`) and upgraded in the asset server's `decode` (`AssetKind::upgrade`), tested from a pack. `mirror:` cooks one clip as another's mirror image by the humanoid map (`clip::mirror`, rest-relative in model space as the retarget is); KayKit's `Dodge_Right` is `Dodge_Left`'s since A1, within 5 cm of the authored one's travel. No track changes: every move table's pin holds, the clip pins move, and the hash test adds a mirrored dodge's table and clip and pins the run's markers. No move reads a side dodge, so no replay covers the mirror. |
| A2 | **FBX curves in deterministic Rust.** Curves evaluated from ufbx's raw keys in the engine's own maths, so an FBX-sourced move may bake tables. **Measure first:** a per-tick spike of the Rust evaluation against ufbx's on a shipped FBX. **Kill if:** any channel differs by over 0.25 mm, or raw keys aren't reachable; FBX then stays presentation-only, as today. **Done when:** an FBX-sourced move bakes tables pinned on three OSes. | M | A1 | **As built (M6 A2):** an FBX's curves, transforms and rests, and the mesh importer's world rests and geometric transforms, are evaluated in Rust from ufbx's raw keys (`import::fbx::curves`, f64 and the `libm` crate, step for step as ufbx does), so a move may bake from an FBX and `BakeError::FbxSource` is gone; spec §5.13's "As built (M6 A2)" has the rules. The spike (`import::fbx::curves::tests`) measured every channel of `animated_clips.fbx` and of hand-written files (every interpolation, tangent, extrapolation, layer blend, rotation order, pivot and other axes and units) against ufbx's own evaluation, tick by tick: the worst is 1e-11 mm against the kill line of 0.25 mm. The fixture's stride pins a table and a clip in `moves::tests`, its spin a cooked set with the root's half turn in `cook::move_tests`, the hand-written files' clips their bits, and two skinned fixtures their imported meshes, all on CI's three operating systems. A property a connection drives is refused by name. ufbx still derives auto and TCB tangents in C as it parses; Blender writes linear keys, and the pinned hand-written clips would show a fused multiply-add moving one. No shipped clip is an FBX: no shipped clip, table, golden or replay moves. |
| A3 | **BVH import.** A reader with a sidecar naming the calibration pose, since capture rarely rests in a T pose; a synthetic BVH generator in `tools/motion`, because agents download no capture. **Done when:** the synthetic BVH retargets onto two characters with its injected slide reported and its bytes pinned. | S | A1 | **As built (M6 A3):** `importer: Bvh((scale:, calibration:, clips: (…)))` cooks a `.bvh` to a skeleton and one clip named after the file, through A1's path (`import::bvh`, stage `bvh` 1); spec §5.13's "As built (M6 A3)" has the rules. The calibration rests the skeleton on a frame (`frame: Some(n)`) or on the hierarchy stood where the first frame stands, and `pose: APose` raises the arms to the horizontal by the sidecar's map; only the rest moves. `tools/motion/capture.py` writes the synthetic walk (A-pose hierarchy, 30 fps, centimeters, noise, a 5 cm slide on frames 34 to 44 of the left foot), committed as `tools/sapphire_pipeline/fixtures/bvh/synthetic_walk.bvh` and held to the generator by `test_capture.py`. Retargeted onto the look-dev character and the warrior, A1's report puts the slide on the left foot's run through those ticks, 3.2 to 4.9 cm above anything else, and the rigs slide 7.5 mm and 2.2 cm a stance of their own (A4's measure first); the file, the skeleton and the three clips are pinned. A malformed file is refused naming its line. Deterministic, so a move may bake from a BVH. Nothing shipped is BVH: no golden, replay or shipped cook moves. |

### Track U: audio (exit test 13)

Audio is a deterministic system over a backend trait: a mock that tests run on, and kira 0.12.6 with cpal on devices, with buses for five categories, cues of weighted sounds that draw a fixed count of random numbers a start (`crates/sapphire_audio/src/system.rs:603-605`), a bar-clocked score, distance attenuation and pan, a slow-motion snapshot, and lines that carry their loudness as a 60 Hz envelope (`crates/sapphire_assets/src/mouth.rs:8-12`). kira has a reverb, a filter, an EQ and a compressor; this engine uses none of them. Every sound is decoded whole into kira's stereo `f32` frames, mono included (`crates/sapphire_audio/src/kira_backend.rs:197-203`), so a mono second costs 384 KB where mono `i16` would cost 96. There is no mix document, no priority or virtual voice, no rooms, occlusion, ambience or streaming, no loudness target, and voices have no language of their own. **The design builds everything above kira, `lewton` and `vorbis_rs`, already in the tree, with no new crate** (§3): an engine voice on kira's `Sound` trait that gives every voice a filter and its authored channel count; the mix as a document with ducking driven by the cooked mouth envelope, since kira's compressor has no sidechain; Unreal's concurrency rules for priority [U: its concurrency reference]; an event tree for cues; rooms crossfading two Freeverb sends; occlusion rays budgeted at layer 5 with a counter of their own, so the simulation's per-step tally (`crates/sapphire_physics/src/query.rs:186`) doesn't move; one audio-IO worker for streams instead of kira's thread a stream; a measured loudness; and voice over from a recording script to a pack per language. Audio is presentation: no checksum and no golden image moves, offline renders are deterministic on one machine and compared with tolerances across machines. Proposed rows [E]: occlusion 0.05 / 0.05 / 0.08 / 0.08 ms; mix load at most 4% / 4% / 6% / 8% of a core; real and virtual voices 64 / 256, 48 / 256, 40 / 192 and 24 / 128; decoded PCM 96, 64, 40 and 24 MiB, in X2's ledger. **Seams:** U4's footstep switches on A6's surfaces; U7 reads the weather's `WeatherAudio` (W8a, Y3), Y2's `wetness` and the hour; U's cues answer W8b's thunder, W15b's `FoliageRustle` and W5's ring and swim events; U12 sits on T1's commands and T18's slot; U13b waits on K2's capped packs; U14 cooks A24's tables into the existing `MouthCurve` format.

### Track G: foliage and forests (exit test 11; drawing after §5's gate)

Every plant today is an entity with a 192-byte instance (`crates/sapphire_render/src/gpu_types.rs:220-244`) at one level (`crates/sapphire_render/src/levels.rs:48-52`), culled with everything else: the hand-placed grove's 14 plants cost 1.05 ms of Desktop-High's GPU frame (spec §5.12), and a forest of 100,000 would spend 1.5 to 1.8 ms of CPU culling before it draws [E]. The foliage family already sways (`crates/sapphire_render/src/foliage.rs:335`) and names a trail (`:101-102`); nothing places, cooks or generates plants, and spec §5.12 still says foliage has no instancing (W0 replaces the line). **The design** is a cooked foliage field per cell, the answer Ghost of Tsushima's and Horizon Zero Dawn's teams gave the same problem [U: their GDC 2021 and 2017 talks]: placement rules in a `FoliageDocument` (`.foliage.ron`) named by the zone's optional `foliage` field, cooked on the deterministic maths into scatter sets (`.sscatter`) of 16-byte instances with stable IDs; chunks culled on the CPU and shared with the prepass; levels with hysteresis and a dithered crossfade; one instanced draw a view, kind and level (§3, "Foliage as a field"); tree levels, tight cards and canopy proxies for shadows; four generated species and ground cover; a pane and strokes for a level designer. **It builds on W and amends it:** W15a's grass is drawn through G2a's field and W16's impostors are G2b's far layer, both written into their rows; W12a's cover channel and W14's density paint feed placement; T13b's brush widget serves G7. **Seams:** wind from Y1; snow from V5's cover functions and wet from Y2 (G4); shed leaves drawn by N's world effects (G5); trunk colliders in the zone's simulation half (G1b); bending from W15b's interaction buffer. Proposed rows (W0): trees near 1.2 / 1.0 / 0.9 / 0.6 ms, beside W's grass and impostor rows; the foliage CPU 0.15 / 0.2 / 0.25 / 0.4 ms; foliage draws at most 150 / 150 / 60 / 40.

Strokes are G7's, so G1a needs no binary document. **G1b (M):** trunk capsules joined to the zone's static colliders and the nav bake, with exclusion around splines, placements and water. **Measure first:** none; a cook-time line per cell in the cook report. **Kill if:** none: it is data. **Done when:** the cook byte-identical on two machines and at 1 and 8 threads; only zones with foliage colliders move their digest, held by a test. | L (G1a, G1b) | W0 (G1a); G1a, W2 (G1b; W12a soft) | |

### Track H: water beyond the lake (exit test 11; drawing after §5's gate)

W2 to W5 plan a painted lake and river, flat at rest, and the engine has no water at all; oceans, shore waves, rapids and falls, caustics, floating things and rain on water were in no row. **The design** keeps one water family (W3's), its kinds by constants over W3a's cooked info texture (bed depth, shore distance, flow) (§3, "Water beyond the lake"). Oceans take at most four Gerstner waves and two detail normals in `sapphire_look::water::{WaveSet, SeaState}`, validated so no wave folds (the sum of each wave's steepness times its number and amplitude at most 1, each amplitude at most a fourteenth of its wavelength [U: GPU Gems' water chapter]), with a Rust twin that floaters, the swim's rendered bob and shore events read; a ring grid follows the camera across the open sea, flat on Mobile-Low; shore waves and swash come from the shore distance. Rivers cook their own polyline with flow and slope, rapids, and falls where the slope passes 50°, their spray placed through N9. Caustics are a generated pattern through the surface on every tier but Mobile-Low, and a dive pass on the desktops. The simulation's surface stays flat and sees no current. **It builds on W and amends it:** W2 (kinds, `WaterSample`, river polylines), W3 (the info texture, a displacement hook, the ring loop), W4 (under water by the fog's uniforms), W5 (ring events), W10 (the rest plane, the body's probe) and A19 (the twin's bob), each written into its row. **Seams:** the sea's share and wind from W8a and Y1; rain on water through W7's ring include; frozen bodies are W2's surface `Frozen` drawn by V6. Proposed rows (W0): the ocean coast 0.7 / 0.6 / 0.65 / 0.5 ms, caustics 0.15 / 0.1 on the desktops, the water CPU 0.05 ms a tier; info textures at most 4 / 4 / 1 / 1 MiB.

### Track Y: weather and wind (exit tests 11 and 15)

Weather is two shares (`crates/sapphire_look/src/weather.rs:19-24`) packed with two lanes spare (`crates/sapphire_render/src/gpu_types.rs:93-95`), and the wind is a renderer struct (`crates/sapphire_render/src/frame.rs:654-680`) the springs never see (`crates/sapphire_anim/src/springs.rs:1801`). W6 to W9 planned falling rain and snow, rain on surfaces, states with lightning and wet characters. **The design** is §3's ("One wind and one weather state", "The frame's uniforms"): one wind field every reader samples (Y1); one weather state that schedules and blends (W8a, W's row amended and split); five shares that rise and dry in world seconds (Y2); a storm sky; hail, dust, far sheets and curl in W6's one draw; wind on hair and cloth; ten presets with their recipe; a pane. Nothing here enters the simulation. **It builds on W and amends it:** W6 (N8a's positions, Y1's wind, the exposure texture), W7 (the ring include, no snow), W8 (two pull requests), W9 (snow dusting) and U7 (`WeatherAudio`), each written into its row. **Seams:** V reads the state's groups and the shares (cover, ice, rings, the aurora, fog density); G, H and N read the wind; U reads `WeatherAudio`. Proposed rows (W0): far sheets and curl +0.10 / +0.08 / +0.08 / +0.05 ms; the storm deck 0.04 / 0.04 / 0.04 / 0.03; wind volumes in foliage 0.05 / 0.05 / 0.04 / 0; the weather's resolve on the CPU 0.01 / 0.01 / 0.02 / 0.03; springs' wind 0.01 / 0.01 / 0.01 / 0.02.

| ID | Package | Size | Depends on | As built |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Y1 | **The wind field.** `WindField` and `WindVolume` (directional, vortex, burst) in `sapphire_look`; `wind.wgsl` holding today's gust term moved verbatim, plus turbulence; the 832-byte `WindBuffer`; one sampler in Rust and WGSL; volumes 8, 8, 4 and 0 a tier on the GPU and 8, 8, 4 and 4 on the CPU. CPU and data: it runs before the gate. **Measure first:** `nature-walk` with 0 and 8 volumes, once W1 lands. **Kill if:** over 0.05 ms on Desktop-High's foliage with 8 volumes; plants then sample at their origin only. **Done when:** every golden byte-identical; the GPU against the CPU within 1e-5. | M | W0 | **As built (M6 Y1, 2026-11-17):** `sapphire_look::wind` holds `WindField` (the global wind, turbulence as a share of its strength, up to 8 volumes), `WindVolume` (draught, vortex and burst as one formula: velocity, swirl and radial flow scaled by a smoothstep from the rim), `ResolvedWind` and the CPU sampler; `shaders/wind.wgsl` the gust term moved verbatim, four stream-function waves of turbulence and the volume loop; `sapphire_render::wind` the 832-byte `WindBuffer` and `WindSettings` (`QualityTier::wind`: 8, 8, 4 and 0 on the GPU as `WIND_VOLUMES`, 8, 8, 4 and 4 on the CPU). Foliage sways in the local air through `wind_local_offset`, beside the unchanged `wind_offset`, behind `FoliageSettings::local_air` (`FOLIAGE_LOCAL_AIR`): at each vertex on Desktop-High, Desktop-Low and Mobile-High, off (compiled out) on Mobile-Low, whose GPU takes no volumes. With its code present, Metal moves a swaying plant's pixels by a code value or two, so the 62 older golden scenes that move pin it off (`--local-air off`) and every golden is byte-identical; `foliage-local-air` draws it, the grove in the stiff wind with the eight sample volumes. `LocalAir::Root` (`--local-air root`) draws only the kill line's fallback's picture: each vertex still runs the sampler, and a per-instance sample outside the vertex stage would be new work. The GPU against the CPU: within 1e-5 m/s at fixed points, times, layouts and caps, at falloffs 0.25 to 0.81 (`--test gpu wind::`). Culling pads foliage by the local air only on tiers that draw it, over the volumes their GPU takes, and the stage takes the tier's CPU volumes from its renderer's settings each frame. The sandbox's `--wind-volumes <0..8>` is the bench's switch; the timed `nature-walk` arms wait for W1 (spec §5.12, the Y1 note). |
| Y2 | **Accumulation.** Wetness, puddles, snow, sand and ice integrated on the world clock by `WeatherSystem`, with their couplings (sleet, freezing, melting, sand clearing, puddles under snow) and drying at §3's rates; re-warmed over the schedule's last six game hours in 120 steps on entering a zone and after a load, so nothing is saved; the frame's `weather` lanes z and w; the rainbow's condition for V11. **Done when:** the shares within 1e-4 of the CPU reference over a scripted day. | M | W8a | **As built (M6 Y2):** `Weather` holds five shares (wetness, puddles, snow, sand, ice); `WeatherSystem` integrates them on world seconds toward the world's dominant state's targets (schedule, region or override; never a cutscene's or the tool's, which are looks), each between changes of state one closed form (`Weather::settle`) from the frame that sees the change, at its target once within 1e-5 of it, with drying at §3's rates (`DRYING`) and the couplings on targets and rates (`Accumulation::coupled`: sleet, freezing into ice, melting only above freezing, rain washing sand, puddles drained under snow over 0.5, the one split found in closed form). The first resolve, which a zone's arrival and a load bring, re-warms from dry over the plan's last six game hours in 120 steps, each split where the dominant state flips: equal to a played day where the shares were near dry six game hours back, which a fast day or a freezing hold breaks; with time of day standing still, six hours of world time of the state as it is. A clock set back holds the shares; a time-of-day jump neither snaps nor re-warms them (owed to Y8). The frame's `weather` carries snow and sand in z and w (no shader reads them; ice waits for V5's `cover`); with a plan the shares lie over the day's keys and the zone's fixed weather (the cook warns of both), volumes, a cutscene and `--weather` over them; with none, the zone's fixed wetness and puddles, the day's keys and `--weather` lie over the shares; a plan whose states wet declares the wet surfaces, and a trip declares for the override and stage plan it keeps. The rainbow's condition for V11 is `WeatherSystem::rainbow`. Proved by a scripted day at 20 Hz within 1e-4 of an independent Runge-Kutta reference at every frame, an arrival and a load re-warming within 1e-4 of it at eight times, tests of each coupling and drying rate, and no golden, replay or digest moved; spec §5.21's "As built (M6 Y2)". |

### Track V: environment effects (exit tests 11 and 15)

Fog is one height fog whose density volumes are "later" (`crates/sapphire_render/src/fog.rs:92-99`), drawn by `fogged` (`crates/sapphire_render/shaders/frame.wgsl:703-712`); time of day already blends looks inside placed volumes (`crates/sapphire_world/src/tod/volume.rs:17`, added at `crates/sapphire_world/src/tod/time_of_day.rs:177`); exposure has no adaptation (`crates/sapphire_render/src/post.rs:287-288`); the desktops' `volumetric_fog` is read by nothing (`crates/sapphire_render/src/quality.rs:249-251`). **The design** (§3, "Snow, sand and ice", "Atmosphere and exposure"): atmosphere volumes placed by the zone with biome presets (V1) and local fog volumes inside the existing fog ray (V2); darkness with carried lights (V3); eye adaptation (V4); snow, sand and frost as `COVER_SURFACES` (V5) and ice as `ICE_SURFACES` (V6); footprints as decal stamps (V7) and trenches in deep snow (V8a, V8b); ambient motes and fireflies as stateless world effects (V9); light beams, moon shafts and flare (V10); rainbows, halos, an aurora and the moon's phases (V11); cloud shadows (V12); froxel fog as a measured tail (V14a, V14b); glare (V15); shafts broken by clouds (V16). V13 is unused: heat haze is one of N11's presets, placed by N9. **It builds on W and amends it:** W8a's states key V's groups; W12a, W13 and W15b carry cover and press; A6 gains the `print` column; T9a, T9b and T15a gain V's markers, handles and partial days, and Y8 shows V's previews; F3's reading decides Mobile-High's sun shafts (§7). **Seams:** the frame's `cover` vec4 is V5's; Y2 owns the shares V draws; N draws motes and haze. Proposed rows (W0), Desktop-High / Desktop-Low / Mobile-High / Mobile-Low in ms: fog volumes 0.08 / 0.08 / 0.06 / 0.04; cover +0.12 / +0.12 / +0.10 / +0.04; ice +0.05 / +0.05 / +0.04 / +0.03; footprints 0.04 / 0.04 / 0.03 / 0.02; deformation 0.12 / 0.12 / 0.10 / none; adaptation 0.02 on the first three; beams +0.05 / +0.05 / +0.04 / +0.03; cloud shadows 0.03 on the first three; the aurora 0.15 / 0.15 / 0.12 / 0.05; volumetric fog 0.50 / 0.60 on the desktops (Mobile-High's 0.4 deleted); glare 0.10 on the desktops.

### Track N: particles (exit test 14)

The effects crate simulates particles on the CPU per owner, capped at 4,096, 2,048, 1,024 and 512 a tier (`crates/sapphire_vfx/src/budget.rs`), as six generated shapes moved by speed, gravity and drag (`crates/sapphire_vfx/src/document.rs:43-130`), with ribbons, mesh layers whose flipbooks turn generated patterns (`crates/sapphire_vfx/src/mesh.rs:121-137`), decals and bends; libraries are documents at version 4 (`document.rs:354`, whose header still says schema 1 at `:1`), and only combat plays them (`game/game_combat/src/cues.rs:232`). Every live particle is extracted with no camera (E1's row). The tiers promise 50,000 and 8,000 GPU particles that nothing reads (`crates/sapphire_render/tiers.ron:184`, `:283`), and the iOS Simulator lacks indirect draws (`crates/sapphire_rhi/src/caps.rs:381-389`). **The design** (§3, "Particles: two worlds and a stateless path", "Effects as an asset kind, and their atlas"): schema v5 and an `Effects` asset kind; forces, noise, wind and ground collision on the CPU; events and sub-emitters; painted sheets in a texture array per library; stepped timing and banded light, the anime look of effects; ribbons and beams; mesh particles; a stateless path for many cheap particles that every tier runs; world effects in a second `VfxWorld`; budgets, levels and counters; a generated preset library and its recipe; a pane for v5; effect lights and sprite bends; a compute spike only on its trigger. **It builds on the plan and amends it:** E1's bound and B5's effect-pixel count (N10 reuses both), F6's second output (N4b and N8b draw into it), T14's pane (N12 grows it), and "What not to do"'s GPU particles row, now split. **Seams:** W6 shares N8a's include; W5's splashes, H3b's spray, G5's leaves, V9's motes and Y7's breath are N9's world effects; Y1's wind reaches particles through N2's Wind force. Proposed rows (N0): combat particles 0.6 / 0.9 / 0.7 / 2.2 ms and world particles 0.2 / 0.3 / 0.3 / 0.8, split from today's one row; the CPU's effects simulation 0.25 / 0.3 / 0.4 / 0.6; stateless particles drawn 24,000 / 16,000 / 6,000 / 3,000; the effects atlas at most 32 / 32 / 8 / 8 MiB; world limits a tier (CPU particles 1,024 / 768 / 512 / 256, effects 64 / 48 / 32 / 24, stateless instances 128 / 96 / 64 / 32, events a step 64 / 48 / 32 / 16, mesh particles 256 / 192 / 96 / 48, effect lights 4 / 4 / 2 / 0).

## 5. Order

213 packages in about 233 pull requests: the performance tracks' 50, and the production tracks' 163 in 183 pull requests. They run in four batches, each production batch beside a performance one. Within a batch, packages that don't depend on each other run as parallel pull requests.

**B0 runs from a worktree left at the tag `v0.2.0`.** Its rounds measure v0.2.0's code, and any M6 change moves what they measure, so they run from that worktree in quiet windows that may take days, and nothing else waits for them: the rest of batch 1 and production batch I land on `main` meanwhile. Only what reads B0's figures waits for them: X4, B9's base rows, exit test 7's baseline and the void-round decision (§3). This replaces the research's proposal that nothing production lands before B0 has run its rounds, which would have held every track on the Mac's quiet windows for code B0 measures at the tag anyway.

### The performance batches

1. **Measurement, and what needs none.** B0 first, from its worktree at the tag. Then B1, B2, B3, X1, K1 and B6, with X4 after B0.
2. **The probes and the cheap measured wins.** B9, with B4, B5, B7 and B8, whose units join its manifest as they land. Then, each on its probe: S1, P1, P2, F1, J1, J2, J3, K2, and X2 beside them.
3. **Each on its probe:** P3, P4, K3 with P5, K4, F2 to F8, E1 to E4, J4 to J7, P6, P7, S2 and X3.
4. **Exit:** Z1, Z2, Z3 and Z4.

### The production batches beside them

| Beside | Production batch | Why there |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | **I. Foundations that touch no frame:** W0, A0 and N0 (the decisions), T0 (when the maintainer has the hour), T1, T3, T4; C1, C4, C6, C7, C12; A1, A3; U1 (its spike first), U11; W2; N1; G6a. | Tools, cook-side and data work move no measured row; the decisions unblock what follows; U1 and U11 lead the longest chains, and a studio needs U11's script early; N1's schema and G6a's species lead the particles' and the forest's chains. |

Each new track lands its slice first, in the slices' order (below); N8b and N9 lead because W5, H3b, G5, V9 and Y7 wait on them. |

What waits on the shader line, terrain or other tracks, then the exit proofs:** A21, then W9, after the shader line; W14 and T13b; W15b; W17b and W17c (on their yeses); W18 (after F7); W11; A12, A19, A24 (after U14); W5 (after A19); T18, T20; U16; G5, G8, G9; H4 (after F3 and F4), H5, H7; Y7; V8a and V8b (after W15b), V14a, V14b, V15, V16; N11, N14 (on its trigger). | Z1 rehearses every exit test, so batch IV's last packages land before it; tests 9 to 15 join Z1's rehearsal and Z2's final run. |

**The gate after batch 2.** B9's and B5's figures can reorder everything after them, as M5's baseline did: a package whose probe kills it lands its measurement, and one whose row turns out larger gains its priority. If B5 reads Desktop-High's shipped fight over its 12 ms line by more than batch 3's priced packages can recover, the plan stops and asks the maintainer (§7): another line for exit test 3, an effects budget for the content, dynamic resolution on by default, or fewer look features (W's, G's, H's, Y's, V's or N's world effects), which only the maintainer may choose (§3, "What M6 is").

### Critical paths

- **Performance:** B0 → B1 → B9 → S1, P1, P2 → P4 → K4 → Z1 → Z2 → Z4. The fight that ships, B4 → B5 → E1 to E4 and F6, starts in batch 2 beside it.
- **Terrain:** W0 → W12a → W12b → W12c → W13a → W13b → W14 → T13b, about five weeks of serial work [E]. Grass and impostors now hang off the foliage field, not terrain (W15a needs G2a, W16 needs G2b and G3), so terrain no longer gates them.
- **Foliage:** G6a → G1a → (the gate) → G2a → G2b → G3 → W16, about four weeks after the gate [E]; W15a follows G2a, and W15b → V8a → V8b come last.
- **Particles:** N0 → N1 → N2 → N8a → (the gate) → N4b → N8b → N9 → N10 → N11, about four weeks after the gate [E].
- **Weather and cover:** W0 → W8a → Y2 → (the gate) → W6 → W7 → V5 → V7, then V8a → V8b in the tail, about four to five weeks after the gate [E]; Y1 runs beside W8a.
- **Water:** W2 → W3a → (the gate) → W3b → H1a → H1b and H2, and H3a → H3b, about three weeks after the gate [E].
- **The shader line,** which gates faces and wet characters: P1 → P2 → P3 → P4 → K4 → P7 → A21 → W9 → W10's character read, so A21 is the latest-starting package of the minimal slices. If P7 is cut, A21 follows K4.
- **The editor:** T1 → T5 → T9a → T9b → T10, and T19 (exit test 9).
- **Animation:** A1 → A5a → A5b → A5c, branching to A7; to A8; to A9 → T17b; and to A14 → A15 → A16a → A16b.
- **Audio:** U1 → U2 → U5 → U6 and U1 → U8 → U9; U11 → U13a → U13b, which waits on K2; U11 → U14 → A24.
- **Waits across tracks:** T17b on A5b and A9; T13b on W14 and T13a; A19 on W2; W19 on C9, on C5; U12 on T1 and T18; W6 on F6, Y1 and N8a; C2 on K1; A10 on J1; W1 on B4; W5 on A19 and N9; W15a on G2a; W16 on G2b and G3; V7 on A6 and N4b; V8a on W15b; G4 on V5; H3b, G5, V9 and Y7 on N9; Y3 on A10; U7 and W18 on W8a; G7 on T13a; N12 on T14; H4 on F3 and F4.

| G4, weather on foliage | 2 (Mobile-Low 1), by constant | the zone can snow or rain |
| H1b, the ocean's grid | 2 (a grid vertex variant and its underside) | the zone has an ocean |

| Y4, the storm deck | 1, a sky variant | the zone's states include a storm |

W's keys come to about 8 to 11 a desktop tier and 5 to 8 a phone tier in a zone with every feature [E]; G, H, Y, V and N add about 24 to 27 a desktop tier (30 to 33 with froxels), 22 to 25 on Mobile-High and 16 to 19 on Mobile-Low [E]; so a zone with every M6 feature builds about 33 to 39 new keys a desktop tier (39 to 45 with froxels) and 22 to 34 a phone tier, against B0's baseline of 73 to 89, and a forest in rain about 15 to 20 on a desktop. Each is a cold compile on a phone; `zero_hitches` passes cold and warm with each moment exit test 7 names, and Z1 times a cold boot into W1's every-feature arm on the simulators.

### The minimal slices

Each track names the smallest set of packages that still answers its area's question. **The slices are the order the tracks land in, not a cut:** every package in this plan is M6's, by the maintainer's decision (§3, "What M6 is"), so each track lands its slice first, in the batches' order and in the order its row of this table gives, and its other rows after it, inside M6. A package another track's slice needs is promoted into that slice (W15a and W16 for G, A6 for V). Sizes count S as half a day, M as three days and L as six to seven days of an agent's work [E].

| Track | Packages (pull requests) | Agent-weeks, full [E] | Minimal slice, in landing order | Agent-weeks, minimal [E] |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T, the editor | 27 (27) | 13 | T0, T1, T3, T4, T5, T8a, T9a, T9b, T15a, T16a, T19 | 5 |
| C, the pipeline | 16 (16; C13 and C14 on their triggers) | 6 | C1, C3, C4, C5, C6, C7, C16 | 2 |
| W, the world's look | 20 (28) | 14 | W0, W1, W2, W3a, W3b, W6, W7, W8a, W8b, W10, W19; and W15a and W16, promoted for G | 6.5 |
| A, animation | 25 (28) | 12 | A0, A1, A2, A5a to A5c, A7, A8, A14, A15, A21, A22, A24; and A6, promoted for V | 6 |
| U, audio | 16 (20) | 11 | U1, U2, U3, U4a, U5, U10, U11, U13a | 5 |
| G, foliage | 10 (13) | 6.5 | G6a → G1a → G2a → G2b → G3, then W15a → W16 | 3.7 |
| H, water | 9 (9) | 4.4 | H3a → H1a → H1b → H3b | 2.4 |
| Y, weather | 8 (8) | 3.3 | Y1 → W8a → Y2 → W8b (W8a and W8b counted in W's) | 1.2 |
| V, environment effects | 17 (17) | 9.6 | V1 → V2 → V3 → V4 → V5 → A6 → V7 → V9 → V10 | 5 |
| N, particles | 15 (17) | 9.2 | N0 → N1 → N2 → N4a → N8a → N4b → N5 → N8b → N9 → N10 | 5 |
| **The ten** | **163 (183)** | **89** | **82 pull requests** | **42** |
| *The performance tracks, for scale* | *50 (50)* | *18* | | |

At five agents at once, the whole programme is a floor of about 22 calendar weeks of perfect parallelism, and 26 to 30 is the realistic range [E], which the maintainer accepted with the scope; the slices beside the performance tracks, about 60 agent-weeks, take a floor of about 12 [E]; dependencies, reviews and quiet windows lengthen both. Terrain (W12 to W14, and W15b), climbing and ragdolls (A10 to A13, A16), the audio pane and packs (U12, U13b), deformation (V8a, V8b), froxel fog (V14a, V14b) and the effects' tails (N3, N6, N7, N11 to N14) are the largest blocks after the slices. With the slices first, an exit test's clause whose package hasn't landed waits for it.

### The ranking

The ranking below is the performance tracks', once batch 1 is in, desktop and phones apart, since the phones are ranked by share. The production tracks are ordered by their batches and critical paths instead, since none of them recovers a row.

| Rank | Desktop (Desktop-High is the tier that misses) | Phones (shares on the M5; a device decides) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | B0 to B3, B5, B6, B9: the measurement | B1, B7, B6 |
| 2 | S1, the shadow latch: the largest measured p95 gain, a few lines | F1, the exact flat path and the interface's fold; then F2 |
| 3 | P1 and P2, received slots and the hull's shadow: the fight that misses | F3, Mobile-High's AO from capsules, up to 23% of its fight if the look holds |
| 4 | J1, J2 and J3: the CPU tails, rows over their lines | J1 (Mobile-Low's worst over its row), J2, J3 with the hint session's workers |
| 5 | P4, one whole-level read, and K4 (not additive with it); K3 with P5 after `df-terms` and `pk-lods` | F7, the scene held under menus (power) |
| 6 | E3, the probe lookup, and E2, the cut-in, after B4 and B5 | K3 with P5, levels by pixel density (4× MSAA at under a pixel a triangle) |
| 7 | F4, one character geometry pass, after B2's serial timing | P2 (a 4-tap tent today) and P4 (about 0.05 ms) |
| 8 | P3; E1; F6 | P7, half floats, the upscaler first; X2 |
| 9 | J4 and J5, the streamer, once the game streams | X3, the phone session, which decides what waits on a phone |

**Load.** At most five agents at once across both programmes, fewer when the Mac is loaded, so production fills the slots the performance work leaves. Agents prove with counts, tests and goldens and hand over their timed scripts; every timed run, B0, B9, W1's units and Z2 among them, is the orchestrator's, alone on the machine in a quiet window with the display asleep. No GPU-cost package lands inside B9's or Z2's timed windows unless its units are in the manifest. At most one simulator or emulator is booted.

**If time runs short: a cut order only the maintainer may invoke.** Nothing in this plan is cut unless the maintainer says so (§3, "What M6 is"); if they ask for less, this is the order, as a last resort, and a package cut this way takes its exit-test clauses with it. The new tracks' tails first: N14; V16; V15; V14b; V14a (and `volumetric_fog` leaves `tiers.ron`); G10; G9; Y7; Y8; H7; H5; V8b; V8a; V12; V11; V6; G5; N13; N12; H4; G4's wet half; Y6's goldens; N11; N7; N6; N3; H6; H2; Y3; G6b; Y4; Y5; G7 and G8. Then the production tail: T20; W11; A12; U9; A18; W4's refraction; T16b; A23; W9; W18; U12b; W15b (only after V8a and V8b); W17c; A19; T7; the terrain block (W12 to W14 with T13b; T13a stays while G7 does); A10 and A11; A16a and A16b; U13b; T17a and T17b. Then the far ends of the slices: V10; V9; V7; V4; V3; H3b; H1b; G3; W16; W15a. Then the performance tail: P7; F5; J7; F7; P6; S2; B8; E4. **Never cut** B0 to B6, B9, S1, P1, P2, F1, J1, J2, K2, X1 or track Z: they are the measurement, the cheap wins that act on the rows over their lines, and the proof; nor W0, A0 and N0 (the decisions), W1 (the unit that measures the look), W2 (the volume swimming and the digests rest on), W8a, Y1 and N8a (the one weather state, the one wind and the one stateless include every other track reads), A5a to A5c (the graph every animation package builds on), T1 (what makes every pane testable), C1 (the rule set artists and CI share), U1 (the voice every audio package needs), or each production area's exit proof.

**Landing:**

- Goldens move only where a row says so: P1's L12 scenes, P2's lit lines across shadow edges, P3's faces if the libm's low bits move them, P4's character scenes, K4's region seams, F2's seven phone scenes, F3's Mobile-High scenes, E2's cut-ins and P7's; W7's `wet-*` scenes, A4's retargeted clips, A7's `lookdev-graph-run` and C8's Mobile-High cloth and spring scenes; each with its FLIP bound in the row. New scenes commit a Metal golden only.
- No performance row moves a checksum; J1 re-baselines Desktop-High's springs determinism tests, which are presentation. **Among the production rows only A14 moves checksums,** by its row, with every affected replay re-recorded in that pull request; W2, W12, A18 and G1b (tree colliders) move the digests only of zones that use them, and no shipped zone does; W1's nature zone is re-pinned by each package that fills one of its areas.
- A cook change keeps §3's rules; K2 lands before any other format bump ships in a patch. The zone's cooked version, the document versions, asset kind numbers, the live-link protocol and the save's version move one landing at a time, in the order the orchestrator lands them.

## 6. Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| **Metal overlaps its passes,** so a pass row is a tail and a saving can hide under another pass or move into the next row. | Gains are judged on the GPU frame first (§2); B2's serial rows split the frame; exclusive costs stay owed to a Vulkan or DX12 GPU. |
| **Gains of 0.1 to 0.5 ms sit inside the run-to-run spread.** | Arms alternated in one sitting, two valid rounds where a verdict rests on it, and B1's per-round tables, so a figure is never a least taken across rounds. |
| **Rounds go void by the display's lock.** | Timed runs only with the Mac idle and the display asleep; B1's significance reading beside the fixed rule; a void round costs a run again, never a false pass. |
| **The shipped fight may be over its line by more than the packages recover** (13 to 15 ms estimated on Desktop-High). | B5 measures it in batch 2, and §5's gate asks the maintainer before batch 3 is planned around a line it can't meet. |
| **The generated cast flatters.** Its control maps are constant per region and its maps are scaled-up paints, so it under-represents what authored texture detail costs, and K4 relies on it. | Rows that rely on it say so; K1 re-runs on authored content; Z3 reviews the benchmark for what it hides. |
| **No phone has run anything,** and the phones' limit (vertices, pixels or bandwidth) is unknown and decides P5, F2, the colour target and ASTC. | Phones ranked by share; no go or kill on a phone millisecond; X3 makes the session one command. |
| **Re-baselined goldens** hide a regression among the moves. | Each row names its scenes and FLIP bound before the work; an exact change must be byte-identical; `render::level_switches` and the testkit's line gates hold lines and edges throughout. |
| **Pipeline count and warm-up grow** with switches and variants. | B6's switches are override constants at their shipping defaults, held by a test; exit test 7 counts every tier's inventory against B0's baseline. |
| **wgpu:** a patch release can move a golden; f16 and serial timestamps may behave differently on the three backends. | X1 lands first with its goldens checked on three backends; P7 is a spike before it is a package; B2's rows are read as splits. |
| **Shader packages collide** in files that include each other. | They land one at a time in §5's order. |
| **The CPU packages touch determinism.** | Springs and restarts are presentation or keep their state; every replay is run with each change on and off. |
| **No artist, animator, level designer, sound designer, VFX artist or environment artist works on the project,** so every production feature is proved on generated content: a project-made character, generated terrain, water, trees and plants, generated effect sheets, stamps and presets, synthetic BVH and FBX, generated test tones and lines. Generated content proves cost and correctness, not beauty, feel or a good mix. | Each production exit test names the judgement it leaves owed; C16's, G8's, Y6's and N11's recipes and U11's recording script are written for the people who come; rows that rely on generated content say so, and Z3 reviews what the generated content hides; §7, items 5 and 8, ask for the people. |
| **Scope:** the production tracks are about five times the performance tracks' work (89 agent-weeks against 18 [E]) and share their five agents; the whole is a floor of about 22 calendar weeks, 26 to 30 realistic [E]. | Everything is M6's by the maintainer's decision, so the plan orders rather than cuts: the slices land first (§5), batches I and II touch no frame and fill the slots the measurement leaves, and the cut order is a last resort only the maintainer may invoke. |
| **The new tracks repeat each other,** each wanting a wind, a weather state, a volume shape or a particle path of its own. | One owner for each shared thing (§2, pillar 14; §3, "Seams between tracks"), every reader named in its owner's row, §5's shared-files table, and Z3's review for a second copy. |
| **The phones' fill and overdraw** under rain, motes, foliage cards and effects is unknown on a tiler. | Opaque grass blades and no alpha-tested grass; Mobile-Low's counts halved before a feature goes (N8b, W6, V9); N10's effect-pixel estimate per world; no half-resolution effects without N10's trigger; a phone's figure stays owed. |
| **Presentation leaks into the simulation** through wind, waves, weather, cover or effects. | None of them enters the simulation (§3); every replay runs with each on and off (exit tests 11, 14 and 15); digests move only where a row says so (W2, W12, A18, G1b), held by a digest test over every shipped zone. |
| **The frame's uniforms grow, or a uniform branch moves goldens,** as L4's did. | `weather` fills its spare lanes and the uniforms grow once (V5's `cover`); cover, ice, the storm sky and foliage's weather are variants or constants, never uniform branches. |
| **Nobody has opened the editor's window,** and its foundations were built and tested headless. | T0's hour at the window comes first; every action is a command, so sessions script, record and prove headless (T1); a pane is judged against a stage capture to the pixel, not by eye. |
| **Determinism where traversal touches the checksum.** A14 changes the checksum's layout and the controller profiles' hash; climbing and swimming add casts to the step; water volumes, terrain and anchors enter zones' simulation halves; and `FixedBody`'s host maths (its IK and FK in floats) may be debt traversal must move to fixed point. | Only A14 moves checksums, by its row, with the replays re-recorded there; the `course-*` replays run on three OSes and arm64 and x86-64; R6's allocation test covers every new query; A14 answers how much of the float maths is debt before A15 starts. |
| **Height fields may allocate:** whether parry's height field gives per-cell triangles and normals without allocating, and whether its seams are stable for climbing, is unknown. | W12b's first day answers it; else the crate keeps the heights itself and sweeps them through the existing triangle path. Climbing stays on tagged meshes until it does. |
| **kira's seams are unknown** (an own sound on a track, send levels on a handle, the cost of 64 sub-tracks and its stream threads). | U1's spike settles them before `VoiceSound` is built, and its kill line falls back to a kira track a slot; every audio package checks for no allocation on the audio thread. |
| **Crates and SDKs wait on yeses** (`objc2-metal-fx`, `dlss_wgpu` and NVIDIA's SDK), as do a cache host and a version-control choice. | Each row says "Waits on §7", and each ask has a default if the maintainer says nothing, so no package waits on silence; TAAU stays the upscaler. |
| **Production work lands during timed windows** and moves what B9 or Z2 measure. | No production package lands inside a timed window unless its units are in B9's manifest; the GPU look features wait for §5's gate. |
| **Shared files across tracks:** the anime character shader, the environment helpers, the temporal resolve, the zone document, the live-link protocol, asset kind numbers and the save. | §5's table fixes the landing order; numbers and versions are assigned at merge, never reserved in the plan. |
| **Cook determinism grows harder:** the shared cache across architectures, impostor bakes, shelter maps and terrain tiles, grass and scatter placement across thread counts, generated trees, river cooks and effect sheets. | The encoder family joins every architecture-dependent stage's key (C10); each bake is byte-identical on two machines in its row; `--verify-hits` recooks a sample nightly. |
| **Store policy for downloaded voice packs** is unknown on both stores. | Asked in §7 before any store build; until then packs ship through the engine's patcher only from a loopback host in tests. |
| **New pipelines and their cold compiles** grow with water, terrain, grass, impostors and SSR, and with the foliage field, the ocean, the storm sky, cover, ice, beams, sheets and the stateless path: about 33 to 39 new keys a desktop tier in a zone with every feature [E]. | Each is built only when a zone needs it, declared in its row and counted by exit test 7 (§5, "Pipelines"); `zero_hitches` runs with each new first moment (a storm, a strike, snow, an ocean, a cave, an aurora, the first world effect and mote); Z1 times a cold boot into W1's every-feature arm on the simulators. |

## 7. Needed from you

Each ask has a default that applies if the maintainer says nothing, so no package waits on silence. §3's decisions are taken with their defaults and are the maintainer's to undo; this list holds only what the plan can't take for them.

**Decisions**

1. **A yes to this plan, and what else M6 holds.** **Given on 2026-11-17:** the maintainer added foliage, water, weather, environment effects and particles, decided that the whole plan is M6's with nothing moved to a later milestone, and accepted it; M6 is ready to start. The calendar this scope takes, a floor of about 22 weeks at five agents and 26 to 30 realistic [E], came with that decision. The decisions most worth a second look, each still yours to undo:
   - M6 is performance and the nine production areas, and the game systems' track waits;
   - the production tracks whole, in M6 (decided): the minimal slices (§5) are the order each track lands in, and the cut order is a last resort only you invoke;
   - terrain inside zones, reopening M5's "terrain out": if no, W12, W13, W14 and T13b drop, and grass places by G1's rules on meshes;
   - GPU particles split: a stateless path allowed (N8), compute only on N14's trigger; the effects atlas as a second texture-array exception;
   - Gerstner oceans and generated caustics allowed; snow's footprints in the slice and its trenches after it;
   - trees with colliders, moving the digests of zones that have them (G1b);
   - eye adaptation within ±0.75 EV; froxel fog only as a measured tail, Mobile-High's volumetric fog row deleted;
   - traversal's tiers: tiers 1 and 2, with grapples and swimming after A16b;
   - ragdolls for enemies on the engine's own solver, presentation only;
   - A14's one re-record of the affected replays;
   - version control for artists' sources: plain git until an artist joins, then a separate content repository on Git LFS with locking;
   - exit test 2's 5% margin, and exit test 3 judging the fight with effects, a cut-in, probes and daylight;
   - exit test 4 reading the CPU tails at their worst frame, stricter than M5's p95;
   - the prepasses row exit test 5 judges (B0's proposal: one depth-only row on Metal);
   - voiding a round on its lock's significance once B0's rounds agree.
